# Appendix J — Verification Log and Open Items (research of 19–20 and 22 October 2026; v3.0 configurability 23 October 2026; v3.1 review 24 October 2026)

> Records how the 514 **[VERIFY]** flags raised while writing the pack were researched and closed, the material corrections that resulted, the outcome of the management decisions of 19 October 2026, and the items that remain open. Every remaining open point is marked in the text as **[VERIFY — open: …]** and listed in §J.5.

## J.1 Method
1. All 514 flags were extracted with their context and grouped into six research areas: domestic ADR (140), international/MSME/consumer/stamp (47), financial sector — RBI/SEBI (63), insurance/workplace/family/education (82), data/technology/telecom (129), tax and fee benchmarks (53).
2. Each area was researched against primary sources (India Code, e-Gazette, RBI, SEBI, IRDAI, TRAI, MeitY, UGC, CBIC, Income Tax Department, Supreme Court and High Court judgments, UNCITRAL, official vendor documentation), deduplicated into questions, and resolved per flag.
3. Seven editors then applied every resolution to the pack, applied cross-cutting corrections wherever they occurred (flagged or not), and reviewed every file end to end for errors and inconsistencies. The master prompt §15–§16 records the binding cross-file resolutions and management decisions.

## J.3 Material corrections made (selection)

| Area | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Mediation Act 2023 | Mediator-as-arbitrator bar cited as s.16 | **s.17** (no party carve-out); confidentiality/recording bar s.22; exceptions s.23 |
| Mediation Act 2023 | s.12A amendment placed in the Tenth Schedule | **Ninth Schedule** (enacted by s.64); not in force |
| Mediation Act 2023 | First Schedule listed "serious fraud, forgery, impersonation, coercion" | Not in the Schedule — Equator policy; actual enumerated bodies added |
| Mediation Council of India | "Established 27 Aug 2026" | Established by **S.O. 4781(E), 27 Aug 2026**; no members or regulations yet |
| Case law | *Arif Azim* dated Oct 2024; *Fatehpuria* cited alone | *Arif Azim* 2024 INSC 155 (1 Mar 2024); *Fatehpuria* 2025 INSC 1409 read with *Rohan Builders* and *Viva Highways* (7 Jan 2026) |
| RBI | Internal Ombudsman Directions 2023 | Repealed; RE-specific **IO Directions of 14 Jan 2026** |
| RBI | Sunday/public-holiday recovery-call bar treated as law | Not in RBI law — retained as an **Equator policy default** |
| RBI | E-mandate AFA-free limit ₹1,00,000 for loan instalments | **₹15,000** (₹1 lakh only for insurance/MF/credit cards) |
| RBI | RB-IOS limitation unverified | **RB-IOS 2026** effective 1 Jul 2026: 30-day RE window, 90 days to complain, caps ₹30 lakh / ₹3 lakh |
| SEBI | Unsuccessful conciliation ₹3,200; three-member fees split equally; LODR Reg 13(3) 21 days | **₹3,240**; **40:30:30**; Reg 13(3) statement in Integrated Filing within **30 days** of quarter-end; SCORES 2.0 from **1 Apr 2024** |
| Insurance | Ombudsman ₹50 lakh cap at r.14; late complaint = ineligible | Cap at **r.17(3) proviso (ii)**; late complaint = **needs condonation (r.14(4))** |
| Insurance | Claim settlement "30 days of last document" | IRDAI Master Circular of 5 Sep 2024 line-specific TATs |
| POSH | External-member fee at r.4 | **r.3**; respondent copy/reply in working days (r.7(2)/(3)) |
| Education | UGC "Redress" Regulations; SGRC 15 days | **"Redressal"** Regulations 2023; SGRC **15 working days** |
| Labour | Labour Codes commencement unverified | **S.O. 5319–5322(E), 21 Nov 2025**; IR Code GRC 30 days is directory |
| International | UAE treated as a reciprocating territory | UAE **not** notified under A&C s.44(b) |
| International | Singapore Convention 23 parties | **25 parties** (UAE from 8 Oct 2026); India signed, not ratified |
| International | Foreign counsel may appear generally | BCI amendment **13 May 2025**: foreign/international law only, clients with principal office abroad |
| Stamp duty | Stamp in the state of the seat | Stamp in the **state of execution (signing)**; Maharashtra award duty now ad valorem |
| Data protection | Respondent data under DPDP s.7; non-resident exemption s.17(1)(c) | **s.17(1)(a)** (enforcing a legal right or claim); exemption **s.17(1)(d)** |
| Telecom | Equator could use a 1600 number | 1600 reserved for RBI/SEBI/IRDAI/PFRDA entities and Government; **1601** Phase I excludes ODR; AI calls are **A2P calls** under TCCCPR Third Amendment 2026 |
| GST | SAC 998216; RCM Sl. 2; fixed ₹20 lakh exemption | **SAC 998215**; RCM **Sl. 3**; exemption tied to s.22 threshold (config) |
| Income tax | Old s.194J/s.195; Forms 15CA/15CB/10F | Income-tax Act 2025 **s.393(1) Sl. 6(iii)**, **s.393(2) Sl. 17**; Forms **145/146**, **41** |
| FX / exports | FBIL 13:30; SGD/AED from FBIL; 270-day realisation | FBIL **13:00**, USD/GBP/EUR/JPY only; SGD/AED via configured cross-rate; GST r.96A **one year**, FEMA **15 months** |
| Payments | 7-day authorisation hold | Razorpay manual capture max **3 days** → capture and auto-refund |
| MSMED | Bank Rate 6% in worked examples | Read from config (**5.75%** from 7 Oct 2026 → 17.25%) |
| Platform | `cases_member` RLS policy permissive (cross-tenant read risk) | Restrictive policy using `core.current_tenant()` |

## J.4 Management decisions of 19 October 2026 — outcome after research

| Decision | Research outcome | How the pack implements it |
|---|---|---|
| M-05 Equator places its own calls via third-party subscriptions | Lawful with the right registrations. Equator cannot hold 1600 numbers; 1601 Phase I does not yet cover ODR; AI calls are A2P calls that must be declared in advance (TCCCPR (Third Amendment) Regulations 2026, No. 05 of 2026, 18 Sep 2026) | DLT Principal Entity; CPaaS as telemarketers; lender-number (1600) mode for regulated-tenant matters; Equator's own declared A2P DID numbers behind flag `voice.equator_service_number.enabled` pending operator written confirmation and telecom-counsel opinion; 1601 watch; consent registration; DND scrubbing; opening disclosure (NN-12, P07, Appendix D) |
| M-06 Equator conducts pre-suit mediation | Voluntary contractual pre-suit mediation and A&C Part III conciliation: **yes, now**. Mandatory s.12A pre-institution mediation: **not yet** — the Legal Services Authority administers it under the PIMS Rules 2018 until four gates close (Ninth Schedule notification; MCI recognition; Central Government authorisation under new s.12A(2)(ii); new rules) | Live pre-suit mediation and conciliation in all verticals; Form-1 PIMS pack for the Authority; flag `com.s12a_equator_authorised` OFF; copy-lint blocks any claim that Equator's report satisfies s.12A (master §16, P08, Appendices A, F, G, I) |

## J.5 Disposition of all open items (research of 22 October 2026)

**What changed.** Before 22 October the register listed 469 open items: J-001–J-170 (v1, §J.5) and J2-001–J2-299 (every `[VERIFY — open]` marker in the v2 files, former §J.6). On 22 October 2026 management sent its own response to the register, prepared with another AI tool, together with the old Equa website as a source for policies.

Each item was then researched again, in eight subject groups:
- workplace;
- commercial and arbitration;
- international, documents and the Dispute Explorer;
- family and tax;
- financial;
- insurance;
- privacy and telecom;
- identity, neutrals and vendors.

Sources were primary wherever they could be reached: India Code, e-Gazette, RBI, SEBI, IRDAI, TRAI, MeitY, UIDAI, CERT-In, court websites, UNCITRAL and vendor documentation. Each item now carries:
- a disposition (§J.6);
- the proposition checked;
- the finding and its evidence;
- the residual uncertainty;
- the approver;
- the production gate or fallback;
- a recheck date or trigger.

**Edits.** Where the pack text was wrong or could be confirmed, it was edited in place: 355 edits across the pack. Confirmed statements carry the stamp `[Verified 22 Oct 2026: …]`, corrected ones `[Corrected 22 Oct 2026: …]`, and provisional ones `[Provisional 22 Oct 2026: …]`. Items that stay open keep their **[VERIFY — open …]** marker, sharpened where useful (for example "(counsel)").

**Limits of this pass.**
- The shared web-search allowance for the session ran out part-way through. **147 items (marked † in the register)** were dispositioned on partial research. They are never marked `verified` on that basis; they carry a conservative interim default and are listed for a second pass (§J.8).
- Several official sites (India Code PDFs, e-Gazette, IRDAI, some RBI PDFs) block automated reading. Where a reputable reproduction was used, the finding says so.
- **This register is research, not legal advice.** Items marked `counsel sign-off` need a written opinion from qualified Indian counsel before the dependent feature is switched on.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| J-001 | CTO / vendor contracting → CTO | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:621: Arbitration of industrial disputes only via IR Code s.42: written agreement in the prescribed form, copy to appropriate Government and conciliation officer. | **verified** (**closed**) | Enacted IR Code s.42 (India Code): (1) written agreement; (2) umpire if even number; (3) agreement in prescribed form, signed as prescribed; (4) copy to appropriate Government and conciliation officer; (5) majority notification; (6) award to Government; (7) Government may prohibit connected strike/lock-out; (8) A&C Act 1996 does not apply. IR (Central) Rules 2026 (G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026) r.20: agreement in Form V, signatories prescribed, arbitrator's written consent attached; Form V copies to conciliation officer and Secretary, MoLE. State forms vary. Evidence: [Industrial Relations Code, 2020 (Act 35 of 2020) — India Code PDF](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22040/1/aa202035.pdf); [Industrial Relations (Central) Rules, 2026, G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026 ](https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/bills_parliament/2026/IR_Rules_2026.pdf); [SCC Online blog — Industrial Relations (Central) Rules, 2026 explained](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2026/05/11/industrial-relations-central-rules-2026-explained/) | Residual: State-sphere forms: UP and RJ rules reported final, MH draft, DL/KA not confirmed; PRS copy of the Central Rules was read via a summariser, not verbatim.. Gate: Flag wrk_ir_s42_voluntary_arbitration stays OFF until (a) counsel approves the Form V template and the Equator s.42 protocol and (b) a verified state_pack row exists for the State concerned; Central-sphere disputes may go live first.. Recheck: Before launch; each State IR Rules notification. |
| J-002 | CTO / vendor contracting → CTO (legal content: General Counsel / external counsel) | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:716: A policy suit-bar clause (e.g. 'no claim after 12 months from disclaimer') is void under Contract Act s.28(b) as amended with effect from 8 Jan 1997, to the extent it extinguishes rights on expiry of a specified period. | **verified** (**closed**) | Verified for policies issued on or after 8 Jan 1997. Under amended s.28(b), a term that 'extinguishes the rights of any party ... on the expiry of a specified period' is void to that extent. The SC held the amendment prospective in IndusInd Bank (2016) 9 SCC 720. A Delhi HC Division Bench (13 Oct 2025) voided a 12-month forfeiture clause in a 2001 fire policy, citing Oriental Insurance v Sanjesh (SC, 2022). Pre-1997 policies follow Sujir Ganesh Nayak (1997) 4 SCC 366. Keep the clock, labelled 'contestable'. Evidence: [Union of India v IndusInd Bank, SC, 15 Sep 2016 (copy on hcraj.nic.in)](https://hcraj.nic.in/sci/2016/3-9087-2016_2016-09-15.pdf); [H P Spinning Mills v United India Insurance, Del HC DB, FAO(OS) 426/20](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/68013102025FAOOS4262009_140932.pdf); [LiveLaw: Oriental Insurance v Sanjesh, 2022 LiveLaw (SC) 303](https://www.livelaw.in/top-stories/insurance-policy-condition-barring-filing-of-claim-after-specified-time-period-void-as-contrary-to-section-28-contract-act-supreme-court-194688) | Residual: Claim-intimation and notice conditions (condition precedent, e.g. Food Corporation (1994) 3 SCC 324) are a separate question, as are arbitration-invocation windows in commercial policies; both need case-by-case advice.. Gate: ins_policy_suit_bar stays informational, labelled 'contestable — likely void for policies issued on or after 8 Jan 1997; act before it where possible'; never shown as a bar to filing. Recheck: Annual case-law review. |
| J-003 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:795: Contractual appeal tier: s.34(3) runs from the final (appellate) award; s.29A application to the appellate tier is unsettled. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Centrotrade v Hindustan Copper (2017) 2 SCC 228 upholds contractual two-tier arbitration; the 2020 sequel enforced the appellate award. No SC ruling found on whether s.34(3) runs from the first-tier or appellate award, or on s.29A for an appellate tribunal; 's.34 runs from the final award' is an inference. Interim: s.34 clock from the final award plus a protective-filing warning from first-tier receipt; separate s.29A clock for the appellate tribunal from completion of the appeal record, with s.29A(3) consent extension in the Appeal Rules. | Residual: Reporter citations from knowledge; Web-search budget for this run was exhausted before this point was searched; reporter/primary text not re-opened this session. Need counsel opinion on s.34(3) trigger and s.29A for appellate tribunal.. Gate: arb_appeal_tier_contractual stays OFF in production (pilot/sandbox only) until written opinion; protective s.34 warning shown on every first-tier award.. Recheck: On receipt of counsel opinion; any SC ruling on two-tier arbitration and s.34/s.29A. |
| J-004 | CTO / vendor contracting → CTO (legal content: General Counsel / external counsel) | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:797: Appendix A note 6: enforceability of policy suit-bar clauses under Contract Act s.28 as amended in 1997 (open for counsel). | **verified** (**closed**) | Closed by the same authorities as J-002. Amended s.28(b) (in force 8 Jan 1997, prospective per IndusInd Bank, 2016) voids clauses that extinguish rights on expiry of a period. Oriental Insurance v Sanjesh (SC, 2022) applied it to a policy claim-lodging time bar, and a Delhi HC Division Bench (13 Oct 2025) applied it to a 12-month forfeiture clause in a 2001 fire policy. Pre-1997 policies are governed by Sujir Ganesh Nayak. Counsel input is needed only for intimation or notice conditions and commercial-policy arbitration windows. Evidence: [Union of India v IndusInd Bank, SC, 2016](https://hcraj.nic.in/sci/2016/3-9087-2016_2016-09-15.pdf); [H P Spinning Mills v United India Insurance, Del HC DB, 13 Oct 2025](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/68013102025FAOOS4262009_140932.pdf); [LiveLaw: Oriental Insurance v Sanjesh](https://www.livelaw.in/top-stories/insurance-policy-condition-barring-filing-of-claim-after-specified-time-period-void-as-contrary-to-section-28-contract-act-supreme-court-194688) | Residual: Intimation and notice conditions; arbitration-invocation windows.. Gate: As J-002. Recheck: Annual case-law review. |

| J-046 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:427: B05: consent award requested after a pre-arbitration settlement (Appendix A B05). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | A&C s.30(2) allows an award on agreed terms only where parties settle 'during arbitral proceedings'. If settlement precedes arbitration there is no subsisting dispute, so a later 'consent award' is open to challenge; no SC authority found. Part III conciliation (s.73 settlement; s.74 award status) is in force today, but Mediation Act s.61 + Sixth Schedule (Gazette; not commenced) will replace A&C ss.61–81. Keep the Part III default and the legal-review flag. Evidence: [Mediation Act 2023 (Act 32 of 2023), Gazette of India, 14 Sep 2023](https://egazette.gov.in/WriteReadData/2023/248775.pdf) | Residual: s.30 text stated from statute knowledge; Web-search budget for this run was exhausted before this point was searched; reporter/primary text not re-opened this session. Need opinion on post-settlement consent award; sunset trigger when Mediation Act s.61 commences (Part III replaced; settlements then under Mediation Act s.27 once in force).. Gate: consent_award_requested_post_settlement behind legal-review flag (OFF by default); default route Part III conciliation.. Recheck: Commencement of Mediation Act ss.27 and 61; counsel opinion. |
| J-047 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:512: POSH Act s.11(4) 90-day inquiry period: start point (complaint receipt vs inquiry commencement). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | POSH Act s.11(4) (Gazette text) reads only: 'The inquiry under sub-section (1) shall be completed within a period of ninety days.' No start point is stated; POSH Rules r.7 is likewise silent. DoPT OM 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III (22 Dec 2016, Central Government servants only) says the inquiry should in no case take more than 90 days; a secondary report reads it as 'from the date of the complaint'. Counting from complaint receipt is the earliest, therefore safest, trigger. Evidence: [POSH Act 2013 (Act 14 of 2013) — Gazette copy hosted by NCTE](https://ncte.gov.in/Website/PDF/A2013-14.pdf); [DoPT OM No. 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III, 22 Dec 2016 (Referencer copy)](https://referencer.in/cs_regulations/CCS(Conduct)Rules1964/11013_7_2016-Estt.A-III1.aspx); [SCC Online blog — DoPT issues fresh guidelines on sexual harassment (3](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2016/12/30/dopt-issues-fresh-guidelines-on-sexual-harassment-of-women-at-workplace) | Residual: No Supreme Court ruling on the start point was found in this pass; High Court views not surveyed.. Gate: Keep inquiry_clock_start = complaint_receipt (Equator policy); show a second informational date from inquiry commencement; never auto-close or penalise on breach.. Recheck: On counsel opinion; before POSH module GA. |
| J-048 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:597: Partition award must be stamped as an instrument of partition (MH Art.12 excludes partition awards) and registered under Registration Act s.17(1)(b) (Lachhman Dass v Ram Lal (1989) 3 SCC 99); post-2020 HC authority. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Maharashtra is verified. Article 12 as substituted by Mah. Ordinance XII of 2024 (14 Oct 2024) still excludes 'an award directing partition', so a partition award is stamped under the partition article. Post-2020 SC authority is K. Arumuga Velaiah v P.R. Ramasamy, 2022 SCC OnLine SC 95 (27 Jan 2022). A document that only provides for a future division is exempt under s.17(2)(v). One that itself divides specific property needs registration. No post-2020 HC ruling on arbitral partition awards was located. Evidence: [Maharashtra Ordinance XII of 2024 (14 Oct 2024) amending Maharashtra S](https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/bills_states/maharashtra/2024/Ord12of2024MH.pdf); [SCC Online blog — K. Arumuga Velaiah v P.R. Ramasamy, 2022 SCC OnLine ](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2022/02/03/not-mandatory-to-register-partition-document-only-detailing-how-the-properties-are-to-be-dealt-with-in-future-sc/); [Law Web (Jan 2022) — registration of award providing for future partit](https://www.lawweb.in/2022/01/whether-it-is-mandatory-to-register.html) | Residual: Whether Arumuga Velaiah concerned a statutory arbitral award or a panchayat award; HC authority post-2020; state equivalents of Art.12 in Delhi/UP/Rajasthan/Karnataka.. Gate: fam_property_arbitration OFF until counsel note; award workflow forces stamp-as-partition + registration task where the award divides specific immovables.. Recheck: On counsel note. |
| J-049 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:768: POSH Act s.11(4) 90-day inquiry period: start point (complaint receipt vs inquiry commencement). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | POSH Act s.11(4) (Gazette text) reads only: 'The inquiry under sub-section (1) shall be completed within a period of ninety days.' No start point is stated; POSH Rules r.7 is likewise silent. DoPT OM 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III (22 Dec 2016, Central Government servants only) says the inquiry should in no case take more than 90 days; a secondary report reads it as 'from the date of the complaint'. Counting from complaint receipt is the earliest, therefore safest, trigger. Evidence: [POSH Act 2013 (Act 14 of 2013) — Gazette copy hosted by NCTE](https://ncte.gov.in/Website/PDF/A2013-14.pdf); [DoPT OM No. 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III, 22 Dec 2016 (Referencer copy)](https://referencer.in/cs_regulations/CCS(Conduct)Rules1964/11013_7_2016-Estt.A-III1.aspx); [SCC Online blog — DoPT issues fresh guidelines on sexual harassment (3](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2016/12/30/dopt-issues-fresh-guidelines-on-sexual-harassment-of-women-at-workplace) | Residual: No Supreme Court ruling on the start point was found in this pass; High Court views not surveyed.. Gate: Keep inquiry_clock_start = complaint_receipt (Equator policy); show a second informational date from inquiry commencement; never auto-close or penalise on breach.. Recheck: On counsel opinion; before POSH module GA. |
| J-050 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:775: Gratuity payable within 30 days — CoSS s.56 sub-section. | **verified** (**closed**) | CoSS s.56(3): employer 'shall arrange to pay the amount of gratuity within thirty days from the date it becomes payable'. s.56(4): simple interest from the due date at a rate not exceeding the rate notified by the Central Government, unless delay is the employee's fault and the competent authority permitted it. s.56(8): appeal within 60 days, extendable by 60. Evidence: [Code on Social Security 2020 s.56 — AdvocateKhoj bare act](https://www.advocatekhoj.com/library/bareacts/codeonsocialsecurity/56.php); [Code on Social Security, 2020 (Act 36 of 2020) — India Code PDF (as on](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/16823/1/aA2020-36.pdf) | Residual: Interest-rate notification under s.56(4) not located.. Gate: None — clock is informational.. Recheck: On any s.56(4) rate notification. |
| J-051 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:796: Appendix A open item 5: IR Code s.4 GRC application window and decision period; s.42 form; CoSS gratuity payment period. | **verified** (**closed**) | All three close. IR Code s.4(5): application within one year of the cause of action; s.4(6): GRC 'may complete' proceedings within 30 days; s.4(8): aggrieved worker may apply to the conciliation officer through the trade union within 60 days. s.42 form = Form V, IR (Central) Rules 2026 r.20. Gratuity: CoSS s.56(3), 30 days. Note pack sub-section numbering elsewhere (s.4(2) for the 10-member cap, s.4(7) for 30 days) is wrong: correct to s.4(4) and s.4(6). Evidence: [Industrial Relations Code, 2020 (Act 35 of 2020) — India Code PDF](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22040/1/aa202035.pdf); [Industrial Relations (Central) Rules, 2026, G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026 ](https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/bills_parliament/2026/IR_Rules_2026.pdf); [Code on Social Security 2020 s.56 — AdvocateKhoj bare act](https://www.advocatekhoj.com/library/bareacts/codeonsocialsecurity/56.php) | Residual: State IR Rules forms.. Gate: None for these clocks.. Recheck: On State rules. |
| J-052 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/A_Workflows_and_State_Machines.md:798: Partition arbitrability among co-owners: generally arbitrable (Booz Allen/Vidya Drolia) but sale under Partition Act s.2 and registration remain court/registration matters — counsel note. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | The pack's position follows the SC tests. Rights inter se among co-owners are in personam and arbitrable. Sale in lieu of partition (Partition Act s.2) is a court power, and registration is for the Sub-Registrar. Arumuga Velaiah (2022) confirms the registration test (present division versus future arrangement). Agricultural-land partition may be reserved to revenue courts by state codes, for example UP Revenue Code s.116. That could make such partitions non-arbitrable under the Vidya Drolia 'statute excludes' limb. That point is for counsel. Evidence: [SCC Online blog — K. Arumuga Velaiah v P.R. Ramasamy, 2022 SCC OnLine ](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2022/02/03/not-mandatory-to-register-partition-document-only-detailing-how-the-properties-are-to-be-dealt-with-in-future-sc/); [UP Revenue Code 2006 (English/Hindi, vaad.up.nic.in) — s.116 'Suit for](https://vaad.up.nic.in/pdf/Revenue_code_2006_englist_hindi_updated.pdf) | Residual: Whether exclusive revenue-court jurisdiction over agricultural holdings bars arbitration in each launch state.. Gate: fam_property_arbitration OFF for agricultural land; non-agricultural property only after counsel note.. Recheck: On counsel note. |
| J-053 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/B_Fee_Engine_and_Rate_Cards.md:866: Whether an arbitral tribunal may shift arbitration costs onto a consumer borrower under A&C s.31A when the loan is on a standard form. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | s.31A(1)–(3) gives the tribunal discretion over costs: costs follow the event by default, and the tribunal weighs conduct and partial success. s.31A(5) makes an agreement that a party pays costs 'in any event' valid only if made after the dispute arose, so a pre-dispute loan clause putting all arbitration costs on the borrower is unenforceable. Consumer Protection Act 2019 s.2(46) 'unfair contract' is an added risk. Equator policy: no pre-dispute cost-shifting clause; tribunal awards only. | Residual: Text of s.31A(5) is from settled statute, not re-fetched (India Code is blocked by the proxy). Counsel to confirm how CPA s.2(46) and RBI fair-practice rules interact with an award of costs against a retail borrower.. Gate: Templates carry no borrower cost-in-any-event clause; neutral guidance asks for reasons on any cost order against an individual borrower. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J-054 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:116: LR-STAMP-007: partition, family settlement and release deeds attract the state partition/settlement/release article (often concessional within family); state-wise articles and family concessions for each launch state. | **provisional** (open) | Partial matrix. Maharashtra: Art.12 (award) excludes partition awards. Mah. Ord. XII of 2024 raised several flat duties, including Art.52(a) release, from ₹200 to ₹500 (primary text, PRS copy). Uttar Pradesh: Cabinet capped stamp duty and registration fee on partition deeds at ₹5,000 (Sep 2025, secondary; notification not located). Karnataka: 2023 amendment set flat per-share partition duties (₹1,000–5,000 urban; secondary). Delhi and Rajasthan were not verified. Evidence: [Maharashtra Ordinance XII of 2024 (14 Oct 2024) amending Maharashtra S](https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/bills_states/maharashtra/2024/Ord12of2024MH.pdf); [TaxTMI/PTI, 2 Sep 2025 — UP Cabinet caps stamp duty and registration f](https://www.taxtmi.com/news?id=54357); [The Realty Today — UP partition deeds capped at Rs 5,000](https://therealtytoday.com/news/regulatory/stamp-duty-on-property-partitions-in-up-now-capped-at-5000/) | Residual: Delhi (Indian Stamp Act Sch. IA as applied), Rajasthan (Stamp Act 1998 Sch.), UP notification number and family definition, Karnataka Act number; whether Mah. Ord. XII of 2024 was replaced by an Act.. Gate: estamp duty calculator shows 'estimate — confirm with Sub-Registrar' and stays OFF for Delhi and Rajasthan until signed off.. Recheck: Before FAM execution go-live; quarterly thereafter. |
| J-055 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:125: Respondent data received from a claimant may be processed under DPDP s.17(1)(a) (processing necessary for enforcing any legal right or claim) for institutional case administration, with s.8(1) and s.8(5) still applying; s.7(e) is the basis for enforcing awards/orders. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Statutory text confirmed: s.17(1) disapplies Chapter II except s.8(1) and (5), Chapter III and s.16 where 'the processing of personal data is necessary for enforcing any legal right or claim' (cl.(a)). s.7(e) covers compliance with a judgment, decree or order. Both commence 13 May 2027 (G.S.R. 843(E) cl.(c): ss.7–17). Open question is whether an ODR institution (not the claimant) processing respondent data is itself 'enforcing' a right or claim, or merely facilitating; no Indian ruling or Board guidance found. Interim: rely on s.17(1)(a) for respondent data limited to the dispute, plus minimisation, security and a respondent notice on first contact (Equator policy). Evidence: [DPDP Act s.17 text (dpdpa.com reproduction)](https://www.dpdpa.com/dpdpa2023/chapter-4/section17.html); [MeitY — Gazette G.S.R. 843(E), 13 Nov 2025](https://www.meity.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/11/c56ceae6c383460ca69577428d36828b.pdf) | Residual: Whether an institution administering the case can rely on s.17(1)(a) on the claimant's behalf; no Data Protection Board decision exists (Board not yet functional per Union's statement to Kerala HC, Oct 2026, reported).. Gate: Lawful-basis register records s.17(1)(a) per purpose; respondent first-contact notice ON; no secondary use of respondent data; marketing to respondents OFF.. Recheck: Before 13 May 2027 or on any Board/MeitY guidance on s.17. |
| J-056 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:144: Whether the 08:00–19:00 recovery-calling window binds NBFCs before the RBC Third Amendment takes effect on 1 Jan 2027. | **corrected** (**closed**) | Yes, it binds now. RBI/2022-23/108 (12 Aug 2022) barred recovery calls on overdue loans before 8:00 a.m. and after 7:00 p.m. That rule is now NBFC RBC Directions 2025 para 100. Para 91(2) applies a narrower 09:00–18:00 window to microfinance loans. The Third Amendment deletes paras 89–100 only from 1 Jan 2027 and replaces them with para 100Y(4) (08:00–19:00). So the window is IN_FORCE today, not NOT_IN_FORCE. Recording (100P) and the GRO footer (100AA) are new from 2027. Evidence: [RBI/2022-23/108, Recovery Agents, 12 Aug 2022](https://rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12378); [RBI press release 2022-2023/707](https://rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_PressReleaseDisplay.aspx?prid=54205); [RBI circular index – Third Amendment Directions 2026](https://rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_CircularIndexDisplay.aspx?Id=13672) | Residual: Paragraph numbers come from the TaxGuru reproduction; the rbidocs PDF is behind a captcha. Whether ODR calls are covered at all is a separate question (LR-RBI-023).. Gate: FIN_CONTACT_WINDOW stays a hard block. Add a microfinance window of 09:00–18:00 until 31 Dec 2026, then 08:00–19:00. Recheck: 1 Jan 2027 (switch microfinance window); re-read RBC MD on each update. |
| J-057 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:162: Whether an independent ODR institution's calls are 'recovery related communication' under the NBFC RBC Third Amendment Directions 2026. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | The term is undefined and appears only in para 100AA. Paras 100P and 100Y bind the NBFC's 'employee / recovery agent'. The larger risk is para 6(11A): a 'recovery agency' is any entity engaged by an NBFC under an outsourcing arrangement to help recover dues, whatever the contract calls it. Para 100A also reaches normal-course collection. If a lender pays Equator to run negotiation or offer-relay on defaulted accounts, Equator could be a recovery agency, bringing IIBF certification (100I), website disclosure (100K) and prior notice to the borrower (100L). Evidence: [RBI NBFC RBC Third Amendment Directions 2026, RBI/2026-27/230](https://rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=13672); [RBI circular index – Third Amendment Directions 2026](https://rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_CircularIndexDisplay.aspx?Id=13672) | Residual: Counsel to decide whether neutral administration, procedural logistics and offer relay fall outside 6(11A), and whether para 100B (OTS carve-out) helps.. Gate: Policy applies NN-12 to all FIN contacts. Offer-relay calls on lender programmes stay OFF until the opinion is in, or run only from the lender's number (X-05). Recheck: On counsel opinion; before first lender programme. |
| J-058 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:164: Whether tribunal costs under A&C s.31A may be shifted to borrowers in standard-form consumer loans (LR-RBI-025). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Same as J-053. s.31A gives the tribunal discretion over costs. s.31A(5) voids a pre-dispute 'pay costs in any event' agreement, which is the typical loan-clause form. RBI's KFS rule (fees not in the KFS need explicit consent; RBC Directions 2025 para 29(5)) does not reach a tribunal's adjudicated costs. LR-RBI-025 is therefore correct as drafted. The open question is the consumer-fairness overlay, not s.31A itself. Evidence: [NBFC RBC Directions 2025 text, RBI/DOR/2025-26/362 (TaxGuru reproducti](https://taxguru.in/?p=1015048) | Residual: Counsel to confirm the interaction with CPA 2019 unfair contract terms.. Gate: No borrower-payable Equator fee in lender programmes (HB) stays; cost awards are shown only as tribunal orders. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J-059 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:182: How the INS engine should treat a policyholder whose claim exceeds the Rs 50 lakh Ombudsman award cap (r.17(3) proviso (ii)). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | The Rules cap the award, not the claim: r.17(3) proviso (ii) sets Rs 50 lakh including expenses (G.S.R. 828(E), 9 Nov 2023), and proviso (i) limits it to direct loss. However, the CIO FAQ says complaints are entertained where the loss payable 'does not exceed rupees 50 Lakhs'. Accepting an award gives up any excess. Equator policy: above Rs 50 lakh, list the Consumer Commission (tier by consideration) and civil court first, and offer the Ombudsman only with a cap warning. Evidence: [Insurance Ombudsman Rules 2017 as amended till 9 Nov 2023 (CIO)](https://www.cioins.co.in/notification/Insurance%20Ombudsman%20Rules,%202017(%20as%20amended%20till%2009.11.2023).pdf); [Insurance Ombudsman (Amendment) Rules 2023, G.S.R. 828(E) (CIO)](https://www.cioins.co.in/notification/Insurance%20Ombudsman(Amendment)%20Rules,%202023.pdf); [CIO FAQs](https://www.cioins.co.in/Faqs) | Residual: Whether an Ombudsman may entertain a claim above Rs 50 lakh if the complainant restricts relief to Rs 50 lakh; the draft 2025 Rules would restructure r.17(3).. Gate: Above Rs 50 lakh the eligibility engine returns 'ombudsman_capped' (warning, not ineligible) and the Consumer Commission or civil court panel is listed first. Recheck: On counsel note, or on notification of G.S.R. 864(E) draft. |
| J-060 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:190: LR-IRDAI-010: MV Act s.166(3) (six-month limit for MACT applications, inserted by the MV (Amendment) Act 2019) is in force from 1 Apr 2022. | **verified** (**closed**) | Verified. MoRTH's notification of 25 Feb 2022 (file title 'S.O. 859(E)') appointed 1 Apr 2022 for ss.50-57 and 93 of the 2019 Amendment Act, including s.166(3). The Delhi HC confirmed that date on 16 Sep 2026. However, on 4 Nov 2025 the SC directed tribunals and High Courts not to dismiss claims as time-barred while the challenge to s.166(3) is pending (Bhagirathi Dash v UoI, W.P.(C) 166/2024; ICICI Lombard v Ayiti Navaneetha, SLP(C) 8412-8413/2023). The challenge was still pending in Sep 2026. Evidence: [MoRTH: S.O. 859(E) 25 Feb 2022 Implementation of Section 50-57 (file t](https://morth.gov.in/sites/default/files/notifications_document/4-SO 859(E) 25 Feb 2022 Implementation of Section 50-57.pdf); [LiveLaw: provisions effective 1 Apr 2022 (ss.50-57, 93)](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/know-the-law/new-motor-vehicles-act-provisions-effective-from-april-1-limitation-for-mact-claims-removal-of-2nd-schedule-more-195450); [Swati v Rakesh Kumar, Del HC, 16 Sep 2026 (paras 27-29)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/40835346/) | Residual: The SC's final decision on the validity of s.166(3); the S.O. number is taken from the MoRTH file title, whose text was not readable.. Gate: mact_6m stays informational: show 'file as early as possible; SC interim order of 4 Nov 2025 bars dismissal for delay pending its decision'; never mark a claim time-barred. Recheck: On the SC judgment in Bhagirathi Dash / Ayiti Navaneetha; quarterly otherwise. |
| J-061 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:204: LR-LAB-006: voluntary arbitration of an industrial dispute requires the prescribed written agreement with copy to the appropriate Government and conciliation officer. | **verified** (**closed**) | Enacted IR Code s.42 (India Code): (1) written agreement; (2) umpire if even number; (3) agreement in prescribed form, signed as prescribed; (4) copy to appropriate Government and conciliation officer; (5) majority notification; (6) award to Government; (7) Government may prohibit connected strike/lock-out; (8) A&C Act 1996 does not apply. IR (Central) Rules 2026 (G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026) r.20: agreement in Form V, signatories prescribed, arbitrator's written consent attached; Form V copies to conciliation officer and Secretary, MoLE. State forms vary. Rule statement is correct. Evidence: [Industrial Relations Code, 2020 (Act 35 of 2020) — India Code PDF](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22040/1/aa202035.pdf); [Industrial Relations (Central) Rules, 2026, G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026 ](https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/bills_parliament/2026/IR_Rules_2026.pdf); [SCC Online blog — Industrial Relations (Central) Rules, 2026 explained](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2026/05/11/industrial-relations-central-rules-2026-explained/) | Residual: State forms for DL, UP, RJ, MH, KA not individually verified.. Gate: Flag stays OFF per State until a verified state_pack form row exists; Central sphere may be enabled after counsel signs the Form V template.. Recheck: Before launch; on each State IR Rules notification. |
| J-062 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:205: LR-LAB-007: s.83 credit within 45 days per Central IR Rules; retrenchment day-divisor. | **corrected** (**closed**) | Corrected. IR (Central) Rules 2026 r.37(1): employer transfers 15 days' last-drawn wages per retrenched worker to the Worker Re-skilling Fund 'within ten days from the date of such retrenchment'; r.37(2): the amount is transferred to the worker's account 'within forty-five days of retrenchment'; r.37(3): employer furnishes worker list and bank details. The 45 days is the fund-to-worker credit, not the employer's deadline. s.70 fixes '15 days' average pay' per completed year (part over six months counted); the 26/30 divisor is not statutory. Evidence: [Industrial Relations (Central) Rules, 2026, G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026 ](https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/bills_parliament/2026/IR_Rules_2026.pdf); [Industrial Relations Code, 2020 (Act 35 of 2020) — India Code PDF](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22040/1/aa202035.pdf) | Residual: s.83 statutory text not read verbatim (PDF truncated); divisor remains interpretive; State rules may differ.. Gate: Divisor stays lawyer-configured (default 26, Equator policy); calculator labels result 'indicative'.. Recheck: On State IR Rules for launch states. |
| J-063 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:210: LR-LAB-012: IR Code s.53(1) proviso, (5), (6) wording. | **verified** (**closed**) | Verified on India Code: s.53(1) proviso — no conciliation proceedings after two years from the date the dispute arose; s.53(5) — report within 45 days of commencement (14 days where a s.62 notice was received), extendable by written agreement; s.53(6) — any party may apply to the Tribunal in the prescribed form within 90 days from receipt of the s.53(4) failure report. IR (Central) Rules 2026 r.23(4): officer uploads failure report within 7 days of concluding; r.23(9): Form II within 90 days 'from the date of the report'. Evidence: [Industrial Relations Code, 2020 (Act 35 of 2020) — India Code PDF](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22040/1/aa202035.pdf); [Industrial Relations (Central) Rules, 2026, G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026 ](https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/bills_parliament/2026/IR_Rules_2026.pdf) | Residual: Rule 23(9) counts from the report date, the Code from receipt; counsel may prefer the Code.. Gate: Equator policy: compute the Tribunal date from the report date (earlier) and show the receipt-based date as information.. Recheck: Before launch. |
| J-064 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:216: POSH Act s.11(4) 90-day inquiry period: start point (complaint receipt vs inquiry commencement). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | POSH Act s.11(4) (Gazette text) reads only: 'The inquiry under sub-section (1) shall be completed within a period of ninety days.' No start point is stated; POSH Rules r.7 is likewise silent. DoPT OM 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III (22 Dec 2016, Central Government servants only) says the inquiry should in no case take more than 90 days; a secondary report reads it as 'from the date of the complaint'. Counting from complaint receipt is the earliest, therefore safest, trigger. Evidence: [POSH Act 2013 (Act 14 of 2013) — Gazette copy hosted by NCTE](https://ncte.gov.in/Website/PDF/A2013-14.pdf); [DoPT OM No. 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III, 22 Dec 2016 (Referencer copy)](https://referencer.in/cs_regulations/CCS(Conduct)Rules1964/11013_7_2016-Estt.A-III1.aspx); [SCC Online blog — DoPT issues fresh guidelines on sexual harassment (3](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2016/12/30/dopt-issues-fresh-guidelines-on-sexual-harassment-of-women-at-workplace) | Residual: No Supreme Court ruling on the start point was found in this pass; High Court views not surveyed.. Gate: Keep inquiry_clock_start = complaint_receipt (Equator policy); show a second informational date from inquiry commencement; never auto-close or penalise on breach.. Recheck: On counsel opinion; before POSH module GA. |
| J-065 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:241: LR-SUCC-002: coparcener's interest devolves on a notional partition before death; wife's inclusion in the notional partition varies by school/state — configuration switch with lawyer review. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Vineeta Sharma (SC, 11 Aug 2020) is verified on sci.gov.in for the s.6 daughter-coparcener rule and the notional partition. Under Gurupad (1978), a widow's notional-partition share rests on the Mitakshara rule that a wife or mother takes a share when father and sons partition. That rule varies by school and region, and HCs differ. No single authority settles it for the launch states. Keep the switch with no default. Show both results until a lawyer selects one, because Gurupad itself gave the widow a notional share. Evidence: [Vineeta Sharma v Rakesh Sharma, SC judgment 11 Aug 2020 (sci.gov.in)](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2018/32601/32601_2018_33_1501_23387_Judgement_11-Aug-2020.pdf) | Residual: Launch-state HC authority on wife/mother's share in notional partition post-2005; not researched to closure (search budget exhausted).. Gate: notional_partition_include_wife has no default: calculator shows both outputs and blocks MSA drafting until a lawyer selects.. Recheck: On counsel note. |
| J-066 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:250: LR-SUCC-011: partition/family-property rights inter se are arbitrable (award stamped and registered where it creates rights in immovables); no post-2020 HC authority located. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | There is now a post-2020 SC authority on the registration limb: K. Arumuga Velaiah v P.R. Ramasamy, 2022 SCC OnLine SC 95. A document that only arranges a future division is exempt under s.17(2)(v). Present division of specific property needs registration. No post-2020 HC ruling on arbitrability of partition awards was found. The flag should stay OFF until counsel signs off. Counsel should also cover agricultural land and revenue-court exclusivity (J-052). Evidence: [SCC Online blog — K. Arumuga Velaiah v P.R. Ramasamy, 2022 SCC OnLine ](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2022/02/03/not-mandatory-to-register-partition-document-only-detailing-how-the-properties-are-to-be-dealt-with-in-future-sc/) | Residual: HC authority; agricultural-land carve-out.. Gate: fam_property_arbitration OFF.. Recheck: On counsel note. |
| J-067 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:251: LR-SUCC-012: partition suits and sale in lieu are civil-court remedies; agricultural land partition may lie before revenue authorities under state law — revenue-court provisions per launch state. | **provisional** (open) | Same research as J2-173. UP Revenue Code 2006 s.116 ('Suit for division of holding') is confirmed from the official UP text. Rajasthan Tenancy Act s.53 is supported only by a secondary source. Delhi, Maharashtra and Karnataka are unverified. Partition Act 1893 s.2 and CPC O.XX r.18 are correctly cited for the civil court. Evidence: [UP Revenue Code 2006 (English/Hindi, vaad.up.nic.in) — s.116 'Suit for](https://vaad.up.nic.in/pdf/Revenue_code_2006_englist_hindi_updated.pdf) | Residual: As J2-173.. Gate: Forum map: generic 'revenue authority under state law' for unverified states.. Recheck: Before FAM agricultural-land routing go-live. |
| J-068 | Regulatory watch → Company Secretary | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:260: LR-EDU-004: UGC Promotion of Equity Regulations 2012 operative because the 2026 Regulations are stayed by the Supreme Court (Jan 2026). | **watch** (open) | Confirmed: on 29 Jan 2026 a Bench of CJI Surya Kant and Joymalya Bagchi J ordered the UGC (Promotion of Equity in HEIs) Regulations 2026 to be kept in abeyance and the 2012 Regulations to operate meanwhile. At the 20 Aug 2026 hearing the stay continued and the Centre and UGC were directed to file replies. No later order found. Evidence: [All India Radio News — Supreme Court stays UGC's new equity regulation](https://www.newsonair.gov.in/supreme-court-stays-ugcs-new-equity-regulations); [International News and Views — UGC Equity Regulations 2026: SC seeks r](https://www.internationalnewsandviews.com/ugc-equity-regulations-2026-supreme-court-hearing-centre-ugc-reply-406507-2/) | Residual: Order text from sci.gov.in not read; case name and next date not confirmed.. Gate: Route discrimination to the 2012-Regulations equal-opportunity cell; recheck before launch.. Recheck: Before EDU launch and monthly. |
| J-069 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:261: AICTE 2019 student-grievance day-count timelines. | **provisional** (open) | AICTE (Redressal of Grievance of Students) Regulations 2019, F.No. 1-101/PGRC/AICTE/Regulation/2019, dated 07.11.2019 (Gazette 19.11.2019 per an AICTE notice). Institutional reproductions state: SGRC sends its report to the institution and student within 15 days of receipt of the complaint; appeal to the Ombudsperson within 15 days of receipt of the SGRC decision. No Ombudsperson decision period found; AICTE notification 13.08.2020 on Ombudsperson appointments. Regulation numbers not confirmed. Evidence: [VCET — Student Grievance Committee (cites AICTE Regs 2019, F.No. 1-101](https://vcet.edu.in/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/student-grievance-comiittee.pdf); [IMDR — Grievance Redressal (cites AICTE Regs 2019 and Ombudsman notifi](https://www.imdr.edu/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Grievance-Redressal.pdf); [MREC — AICTE Ombudsperson notification 13.08.2020 (Regs published in G](https://www.mrec.ac.in/MREC_GRIEVANCE/Ombudsman.pdf) | Residual: Gazette text of the 2019 Regulations not read; regulation numbers and Ombudsperson period unconfirmed. (The superseded 2012 AICTE Regs had 10-day committee and one-month Ombudsman limits — do not use.). Gate: Equator policy for EDU_AICTE: SGRC 15 calendar days from receipt; appeal 15 days; Ombudsperson target 30 days labelled 'Equator target', not statutory.. Recheck: Before EDU launch. |
| J-070 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:270: LR-CONS-004: no appeal lies against a consumer-mediation settlement order under CPA 2019 s.81(1); the bar sits in provisos to ss.41, 51 and 67. | **provisional** (open) | Best answer from the statute as known, not re-read this pass: each appeal section has a proviso that no appeal lies from an order under s.81(1) passed under a mediated settlement under s.80. These are s.41 (District to State), s.51 (State to National) and s.67 (National to SC). Reference to mediation is s.37, applied to the State and National Commissions by ss.49 and 59. India Code could not be read (robots-blocked). Equator copy: 'mediated settlement order is final; no appeal', with no proviso number. Evidence: [India Code: Consumer Protection Act 2019 (record page; section text no](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/handle/123456789/15256) | Residual: Proviso ordinal (second/third) in each section; s.49/s.59 cross-application wording.. Gate: UI copy states finality without proviso numbers until the citation is confirmed. Recheck: Lead to confirm on India Code in the next pass (non-blocking). |
| J-071 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:271: LR-CONS-005: e-Jagriti (DoCA) is the consumer filing portal and successor to e-Daakhil; exact launch date open. | **provisional** (open) | The portal at https://e-jagriti.gov.in/ is live and identifies itself as a Department of Consumer Affairs platform (checked 22 Oct 2026). The page shows no launch date, and the date could not be confirmed from a primary source in this pass because web search was exhausted. The 7 Sep 2020 date in the pack is e-Daakhil's launch, not e-Jagriti's, and the sentence should say so. This is non-blocking: deep-link the live portal and drop the launch date from user-facing copy. Evidence: [e-Jagriti portal](https://e-jagriti.gov.in/) | Residual: e-Jagriti go-live date (PIB/DoCA press release).. Gate: None (non-blocking); no launch date in UI copy. Recheck: Next research pass. |
| J-072 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:297: No number series is designated for ODR/legal services' own service calls as of Oct 2026; Equator uses declared fixed-line/SIP DID ranges pending 1601 notification; operator confirmation and counsel opinion required. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Confirmed. TRAI PR 91/2026 (10 Jul 2026) clarified that 1600 is reserved for RBI/SEBI/IRDAI/PFRDA-regulated entities and Government. TRAI's 10 Aug 2026 Direction and PR 113/2026 limit 1601 Phase I to utilities and courier/logistics. Numbers go directly to eligible entities, with 90 days for onboarding. No later phase naming legal or ODR services was found. The Third Amendment (reg 4) requires A2P declaration of CLI ranges, and reg 35A exempts 140xx/1600xx/1601xx from the A2P termination charge. Whether Equator's DID-based service calls are permitted remains a question for the operator and counsel. Evidence: [TRAI PR No. 91/2026](https://trai.gov.in/sites/default/files/2026-07/PR_No91of2026.pdf); [SCC Online — TRAI clarifies 1600 and 140 series (18 Jul 2026)](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2026/07/18/trai-clarifies-1600-and-140-series-number-framework/); [ThePrint — TRAI directs 1601 series for utilities, logistics (10 Aug 2](https://theprint.in/economy/trai-directs-use-of-1601-series-for-service-transactional-calls-by-utilities-logistics-cos/3010594/) | Residual: Operator acceptance of DID CLIs for Equator's category; 1601 Phase II scope and timing.. Gate: voice.equator_service_number.enabled OFF until operator written confirmation and telecom-counsel opinion are on file.. Recheck: Monthly TRAI press-release check; on any 1601 phase notification. |
| J-073 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:301: IT Rules 2021 r.3 as amended (G.S.R. 120(E), in force 20 Feb 2026): grievance acknowledgement 24 h, disposal 7 days, takedown 3 h of a lawful order, SGI labelling; whether Equator is an intermediary for party-uploaded content. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | G.S.R. number confirmed: G.S.R. 120(E), 10 Feb 2026, in force 20 Feb 2026. It cuts grievance disposal under r.3(2)(a)(i) from 15 to 7 days and r.3(1)(d) takedown from 36 hours to 3 hours, and adds SGI labelling (r.3(3)). The pack's timelines match. Whether Equator is an 'intermediary' (IT Act s.2(1)(w): receives, stores or transmits records on behalf of another) for party uploads is a legal judgement. Hosting party evidence arguably qualifies. Equator's own AI output is first-party content, so Equator is not an intermediary for it. Keep compliance as the default. Evidence: [MeitY — Gazette G.S.R. 120(E), 10 Feb 2026](https://www.meity.gov.in/static/uploads/2026/02/f55fe52418b03f58b0669f6a8bc03b6d.pdf); [JSA Prism — IT Intermediary Amendment 2026](https://www.jsalaw.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/JSA-Prism-Infotech-February-2026-IT-Intermediary-Amendment-2026.Final_.pdf) | Residual: Intermediary characterisation of a closed-access ODR case file (not a public platform); safe-harbour (s.79) consequences.. Gate: Intermediary due diligence stays ON as the safe default (grievance officer, 24 h/7 d, 3 h takedown runbook, SGI flagging of uploads).. Recheck: On counsel opinion; next IT Rules amendment. |
| J-074 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:353: Carriage of Goods by Sea Act 2025 Schedule (Hague-Visby Rules) Art. III(6) carries the one-year suit time bar. | **corrected** (**closed**) | Closed by J2-235: India Code text of the Carriage of Goods by Sea Act 2025 (Act 19 of 2025) places the one-year time bar in Schedule Art. III para 6(c); LR-INT-023 in Appendix G now cites para 6(c) and is marked verified. Evidence: [Carriage of Goods by Sea Act 2025 (Act 19 of 2025), India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/21917/1/A2025-19.pdf) | Residual: None for numbering; computation convention under J2-240.. Gate: hague_visby_1y clock ON. Recheck: Next India Code check. |
| J-075 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P01_Identity_Login_Onboarding_Consent.md:143: Under the BCI Certificate and Place of Practice (Verification) Rules 2015 a Certificate of Practice (CoP) is renewed/verified on a 5-year cycle. | **verified** (**closed**) | Confirmed. r.9.1 of the 2015 Rules: a CoP 'shall be valid for a period of five years only and is liable to be verified every five years', by an application filed up to six months before expiry (Form A; r.9.2). Late applications attract a late fee/penalty (rr.10-11); failure to apply raises a presumption that the advocate has left practice (rr.17, 20.1). The 2014 'renewal' rules were repealed, so the correct term is 'verification', not 'renewal'. Equator's annual re-verification is stricter Equator policy and can stay. Evidence: [BCI Certificate and Place of Practice (Verification) Rules 2015 — text](https://www.casemine.com/act/in/5f5f114e9fca195c05cce24a); [LiveLaw — Certificate of Practice Rules 2015: An Analysis](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/certificate-practice-rules-2015-analysis); [LiveLaw — SC stays High Court proceedings against the Verification Rul](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/supreme-court-stays-bar-councils-verification-rules) | Residual: Text read from a CaseMine reproduction; keep a BCI-published copy on file. The 2015 transfer petitions in the Supreme Court stayed High Court challenges, not the Rules.. Gate: None — CoP expiry date captured as 'valid until' = issue/last verification + 5 years; Equator still re-verifies annually.. Recheck: Any BCI amendment to the 2015 Rules. |
| J-076 | CTO / vendor contracting → CTO | phases/P01_Identity_Login_Onboarding_Consent.md:737: (a) CoP verification cycle is 5-yearly; (b) an official online roll exists per State Bar Council before any lookupBarRoll adapter is enabled. | **vendor confirmation** (open) | (a) Verified: r.9.1 of the 2015 Rules — CoP valid 5 years, verified every 5 years. (b) Some State Bar Councils publish an official lookup (e.g., Bar Council of Delhi 'Enrolment Status' portal; Maharashtra & Goa and Karnataka reportedly offer advocate search), but none publishes an API or terms permitting automated querying, and the Delhi portal's exposure of addresses, mobiles and DOB was challenged in the Delhi High Court in Sept 2026 (treated as a representation, 8 weeks to decide). Automated lookup should need each Council's written permission. Evidence: [BCI CoP Rules 2015 — text (CaseMine)](https://www.casemine.com/act/in/5f5f114e9fca195c05cce24a); [LiveLaw — Delhi HC on Bar Council of Delhi enrolment-status portal dis](https://www.livelaw.in/high-court/delhi-high-court/delhi-bar-council-advocates-personal-data-disclosed-enrolment-status-portal-548289); [Jurigram — state-wise advocate enrolment search (secondary)](https://jurigram.com/blog/legal-career/advocate-enrollment-number-search-state-wise-how-clients-can-verify-lawyers) | Residual: Per-State list of official portals not individually confirmed on each Council's site; no Council terms of use for automated access found. Outcome of the BCD representation unknown.. Gate: lookupBarRoll OFF for every State; staff-assisted check on the official portal (screenshot + URL + timestamp into evidence) plus two-person review.. Recheck: Per State on receipt of written permission/API; BCD decision on masking (due ~Nov 2026). |
| J-077 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P01_Identity_Login_Onboarding_Consent.md:739: PML (Maintenance of Records) Rules 2005 r.9(3) beneficial-ownership threshold is 10% (introduced by the Amendment Rules of 7 Mar 2023); G.S.R. number to be confirmed. | **provisional** (open) | FIU-IND's consolidated text of the PML (Maintenance of Records) Rules 2005 confirms the threshold. Under r.9(3)(a), 'controlling ownership interest' in a company means more than 10% of shares, capital or profits. r.9(3)(b) applies a 10% test to partnership firms. The FIU text does not show which amending notification made the change, or its G.S.R. number. That number was not confirmed today (search budget exhausted), so do not cite one. Equator is not a PMLA reporting entity, so adopt the 10% test as Equator policy. Evidence: [FIU-IND — PML (Maintenance of Records) Rules 2005 (consolidated)](https://fiuindia.gov.in/files/AML_Legislation/notification.html); [FIU-IND — PMLA Rules PDF](https://fiuindia.gov.in/pdfs/AML_legislation/PMLA_2005.pdf) | Residual: G.S.R. number and date of the 2023 amendment (believed 7 Mar 2023, unconfirmed); counsel confirmation that adoption as policy creates no PMLA obligation.. Gate: BO capture at >10% stays ON as Equator policy; cite 'r.9(3)(a), as amended' without G.S.R. number.. Recheck: Next egazette search window. |
| J-078 | Legal counsel → CTO | phases/P01_Identity_Login_Onboarding_Consent.md:742: Meta WhatsApp authentication-template rules applicable to India at WABA set-up. | **vendor confirmation** (open) | Meta developer docs require an authentication template for OTPs sent via WhatsApp. The text is fixed and preset, with an optional security recommendation and a code-expiry footer (1–90 minutes). Buttons are one-tap autofill, copy-code, or zero-tap (Android only). On Cloud API, codes go only to the primary device. India is on the 'authentication-international' rate list, but a business is charged the domestic rate in its primary business location. Equator, India-based, pays the standard India authentication rate if its primary business location is verified as India. No India-only content rule was found. TRAI DLT does not apply to WhatsApp. Evidence: [Meta — Authentication templates](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/whatsapp/business-management-api/authentication-templates/); [Meta — Authentication-international rates](https://developers.facebook.com/docs/whatsapp/pricing/authentication-international-rates/) | Residual: BSP-specific onboarding rules; verification of primary_business_location = IN.. Gate: WhatsApp OTP OFF until template approved and primary business location verified as India; SMS OTP via DLT is the fallback.. Recheck: At WABA set-up. |
| J-079 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P02_Case_Core_Intake_Forms_Routing_Notices_DMS.md:780: How the RB-IOS 2026 30-day and 90-day periods are computed. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | RB-IOS 2026 cl.10(1)(f) lets a complaint go to the Ombudsman if the RE has not replied within 30 days of receiving it, or any longer RBI/NPCI/card-network time, 'whichever is higher'. Cl.10(1)(g) requires filing within 90 days of that date or of the RE's last communication, whichever is later. The Scheme does not define 'day' and has no computation rule. Cl.3(2) imports meanings from the parent Acts. The FAQ is silent too. Equator policy: calendar days, trigger day excluded, no roll-forward, and a warning 7 days before the deadline. Evidence: [RB-IOS 2026 text (copy hosted by Ujjivan SFB)](https://www.ujjivansfb.bank.in/assets/1_RESERVE_BANK_INTEGRATED_OMBUDSMAN_SCHEME_2026_9f0db1d061.pdf); [RBI RB-IOS 2026 FAQ](https://www.rbi.org.in/commonman/Upload/English/FAQs/PDFs/RBIOS01072026.pdf) | Residual: Whether General Clauses Act s.9/s.10 (exclusion of the first day; holiday roll-forward) applies to an RBI scheme. The Annex says 'whichever is later' where cl.10(1)(f) says 'whichever is higher'.. Gate: The clock is advisory (signposting) and never blocks filing; periods stay as config (X-13). Recheck: On counsel opinion; on any RB-IOS amendment. |
| J-080 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P03_Neutrals_Directory_Marketplace_Empanelment_Allocation.md:68: BCI CoP Rules 2015 verification cycle is 5-yearly (P03 credential table). | **verified** (**closed**) | Confirmed by r.9.1 of the 2015 Rules: CoP valid for five years and liable to be verified every five years; application for verification within six months before expiry. The P03 table's 're-verify at term renewal' and annual CoP checks are stricter Equator policy and consistent with the Rules. Evidence: [BCI CoP Rules 2015 — text (CaseMine)](https://www.casemine.com/act/in/5f5f114e9fca195c05cce24a); [LiveLaw — Certificate of Practice Rules 2015: An Analysis](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/certificate-practice-rules-2015-analysis) | Residual: Keep a BCI-published copy on file.. Gate: None. Recheck: Any BCI amendment. |
| J-081 | Legal counsel → CTO | phases/P03_Neutrals_Directory_Marketplace_Empanelment_Allocation.md:68: An official online roll exists per State Bar Council so that lookupBarRoll can be enabled per State (P03 credential table). | **vendor confirmation** (open) | Partly. Official lookups exist in some States (Bar Council of Delhi 'Enrolment Status' portal confirmed by Delhi HC proceedings, Sept 2026; Maharashtra & Goa and Karnataka reportedly offer advocate search). None publishes an API or terms allowing automated queries; querying their web forms by script is scraping in substance. | J-082 † | Legal counsel → CTO | phases/P03_Neutrals_Directory_Marketplace_Empanelment_Allocation.md:70: ICAI/ICSI/ICMAI provide public member and CoP verification portals. | **vendor confirmation** (open) | ICAI's 'Trace a Member' (traceamember.icai.org) requires the searcher's own membership number (MRN) and a captcha, so it is a member-to-member tool, not a public verification service, and cannot be automated. ICSI's site did not render content for automated reading; ICMAI not checked (search budget exhausted). Default: member uploads CoP certificate; verification by institute letter or a staff member who is an institute member using the official portal; annual CoP check. Evidence: [ICAI — Trace a Member](https://traceamember.icai.org/) | Residual: ICSI and ICMAI portals.. Gate: No automated institute lookups. Recheck: Before neutral empanelment opens. |
| J-083 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P03_Neutrals_Directory_Marketplace_Empanelment_Allocation.md:619: OI-03: may ratings and video appear on advocate-neutral profiles consistent with BCI r.36? | **counsel sign-off** (open) | On the Schedule to the 2008 r.36 proviso, ratings, reviews, video and fee information are not listed and 'any additional input' is deemed a violation. Courts and BCI tightened enforcement against online listings in 2024 (Madras HC, P.N. Vignesh; BCI cease-and-desist to Sulekha, JustDial, Quikr, Grotal). An arguable distinction exists for a profile describing the person's role as an empanelled neutral (not offering legal services), but no authority supports it. Keep ratings/video OFF on advocate profiles pending a written opinion. Evidence: [Supreme Court AOR exam material — r.36 2008 amendment](https://main.sci.nic.in/pdf/aorexam/10122021_070532.pdf); [LawyersClubIndia — Schedule of permitted website information](https://www.lawyersclubindia.com/articles/web-information-shedule-prescribed-to-advocates-by-bci-amen-1657.asp); [SCC Online — BCI directive after Madras HC (Jul 2024)](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2024/07/09/bci-bans-illegal-solicitation-of-legal-services-by-lawyers-on-online-portals-after-madras-hc-decision/) | Residual: Neutral-panel exception untested; SC appeals pending.. Gate: No ratings or video on advocate-neutral profiles. Recheck: Counsel opinion; SC outcome in Sulekha SLP. |

| J-085 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P04_Resolution_Engines_Virtual_Hearings.md:132: s.30 presupposes a subsisting dispute 'during arbitral proceedings', so the 'sign an arbitration agreement and request a consent award' variant is doubtful. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | A&C s.30(2) allows an award on agreed terms only where parties settle 'during arbitral proceedings'. If settlement precedes arbitration there is no subsisting dispute, so a later 'consent award' is open to challenge; no SC authority found. Part III conciliation (s.73 settlement; s.74 award status) is in force today, but Mediation Act s.61 + Sixth Schedule (Gazette; not commenced) will replace A&C ss.61–81. Evidence: [Mediation Act 2023 (Act 32 of 2023), Gazette of India, 14 Sep 2023](https://egazette.gov.in/WriteReadData/2023/248775.pdf) | Residual: Web-search budget for this run was exhausted before this point was searched; reporter/primary text not re-opened this session. Recheck: Counsel opinion; Mediation Act s.61 commencement. |
| J-086 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P04_Resolution_Engines_Virtual_Hearings.md:593: Arb-med: an unsigned sealed award text is not an award 'made' for s.29A because s.31(1) requires signature. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | The proposition follows the statutory text: s.31(1) requires the award to be in writing and signed by the tribunal members, and s.29A(1) requires the award to be 'made' within 12 months of completion of pleadings (+6 by consent). A sealed unsigned draft is therefore not a made award; no authority found treating it otherwise. The design guard (mediation ends ≥ 15 days before the s.29A due date; signature on unsealing) is the correct conservative approach. Note Mediation Act s.17(a) (Gazette) bars a statutory-mode mediator from later acting as arbitrator. Evidence: [Mediation Act 2023 (Act 32 of 2023), Gazette of India, 14 Sep 2023](https://egazette.gov.in/WriteReadData/2023/248775.pdf) | Residual: A&C text from statute knowledge; Web-search budget for this run was exhausted before this point was searched; reporter/primary text not re-opened this session.. Gate: Hard guard: unseal-and-sign must complete before s.29A due date; if within 15 days, mediation cannot start without s.29A(3) consent extension on record.. Recheck: Counsel opinion. |

Keep the Part III default and the legal-review flag. | J-088 † | Legal counsel → CTO | phases/P05_Fees_Payments_Escrow_MultiCurrency.md:678: NPCI UPI AutoPay mandate caps and retry rules. | **vendor confirmation** (open) | Secondary sources say NPCI OC-215/2025-26 (21 May 2025, effective 1 Aug 2025) allows AutoPay execution only outside peak hours (peak: 10:00–13:00 and 17:00–21:30) and permits one attempt plus three retries per execution. The NPCI circular itself was not opened. The no-AFA limits (₹15,000; ₹1 lakh for insurance, MF and card bills) come from the RBI e-mandate framework, which the pack already records. | J-089 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P06_Outcomes_Drafting_eSign_eStamp_Awards_Enforcement.md:70: CIC account-status codes for a compromise settlement (PT-FIN-03). | **verified** (**closed**) | UCRF v3.74 (in force 1 Jul 2026) Tag 22 'Credit Facility Status' (formerly 'Written-off and Settled Status') codes: 03 Settled, 04 Post (WO) Settled, 13 Post Write Off Closed, 15 Auctioned & Settled, 16 Repossessed & Settled, 99 Clear existing status. Tag 43 'Settlement Amount' is reported only with 03/04/15/16. A normally closed account carries Date Closed and a zero balance with no status. So 'settled' and 'closed' are distinct reported states, and PT-FIN-03's block is correct. Evidence: [Experian – Consumer UCRF Version 3.74 (w.e.f. 1 Jul 2026)](https://www.experian.in/wp-content/uploads/sites/39/2026/07/Consumer-UCRF-Version-3.74.pdf) | Residual: The UCRF is the CIC data-format specification (Experian copy), not an RBI instrument. Code lists change by version.. Gate: PT-FIN-03 block stays; templates name the UCRF status the lender undertakes to report. Recheck: Each UCRF version change. |

| J-095 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P07_Omnichannel_Communications_AI_Voice.md:723: Whether an Equator offer-relay call is a 'recovery communication' under the RBC Third Amendment Directions 2026. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | The pack's reading of paras 100P, 100Y and 100AA is accurate, but it misses para 6(11A) and its Explanation. A recovery agency is any entity engaged by an NBFC under an outsourcing arrangement to help recover loan dues, whatever the contract calls it. Para 100A extends the Section to 'collection of dues in the normal course'. A lender-paid offer relay on overdue accounts is the strongest case for coverage. Recording and treating the calls as recovery-related (current default) is right. Counsel must also rule on recovery-agency status. Evidence: [RBI NBFC RBC Third Amendment Directions 2026, RBI/2026-27/230](https://rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=13672); [RBI circular index – Third Amendment Directions 2026](https://rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_CircularIndexDisplay.aspx?Id=13672) | Residual: Whether neutral administration is outsourcing 'to help recover'; whether para 100B (OTS carve-out) applies.. Gate: Offer-relay calls recorded, inside the window, GRO footer; lender-number mode only (X-05). Recheck: On counsel opinion. |

| J-101 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P09_Enterprise_GRM_POSH_SaaS.md:140: AICTE 2019 student-grievance day-count timelines. | **provisional** (open) | AICTE (Redressal of Grievance of Students) Regulations 2019, F.No. 1-101/PGRC/AICTE/Regulation/2019, dated 07.11.2019 (Gazette 19.11.2019 per an AICTE notice). No start point is stated; POSH Rules r.7 is likewise silent. DoPT OM 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III (22 Dec 2016, Central Government servants only) says the inquiry should in no case take more than 90 days; a secondary report reads it as 'from the date of the complaint'. Counting from complaint receipt is the earliest, therefore safest, trigger. Evidence: [POSH Act 2013 (Act 14 of 2013) — Gazette copy hosted by NCTE](https://ncte.gov.in/Website/PDF/A2013-14.pdf); [DoPT OM No. 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III, 22 Dec 2016 (Referencer copy)](https://referencer.in/cs_regulations/CCS(Conduct)Rules1964/11013_7_2016-Estt.A-III1.aspx); [SCC Online blog — DoPT issues fresh guidelines on sexual harassment (3](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2016/12/30/dopt-issues-fresh-guidelines-on-sexual-harassment-of-women-at-workplace) | Residual: No Supreme Court ruling on the start point was found in this pass; High Court views not surveyed.. Gate: Keep inquiry_clock_start = complaint_receipt (Equator policy); show a second informational date from inquiry commencement; never auto-close or penalise on breach.. Recheck: On counsel opinion; before POSH module GA. |

Gate: state_pack rows stay verified_on = null; packs show Central text plus 'confirm State form' banner.. Recheck: 31 Dec 2026 and on each State notification. |
| J-105 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P09_Enterprise_GRM_POSH_SaaS.md:709: POSH Act s.11(4) 90-day inquiry period: start point (complaint receipt vs inquiry commencement). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | POSH Act s.11(4) (Gazette text) reads only: 'The inquiry under sub-section (1) shall be completed within a period of ninety days.' No start point is stated; POSH Rules r.7 is likewise silent. DoPT OM 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III (22 Dec 2016, Central Government servants only) says the inquiry should in no case take more than 90 days; a secondary report reads it as 'from the date of the complaint'. Counting from complaint receipt is the earliest, therefore safest, trigger. Evidence: [POSH Act 2013 (Act 14 of 2013) — Gazette copy hosted by NCTE](https://ncte.gov.in/Website/PDF/A2013-14.pdf); [DoPT OM No. 11013/7/2016-Estt.A-III, 22 Dec 2016 (Referencer copy)](https://referencer.in/cs_regulations/CCS(Conduct)Rules1964/11013_7_2016-Estt.A-III1.aspx); [SCC Online blog — DoPT issues fresh guidelines on sexual harassment (3](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2016/12/30/dopt-issues-fresh-guidelines-on-sexual-harassment-of-women-at-workplace) | Residual: No Supreme Court ruling on the start point was found in this pass; High Court views not surveyed.. Gate: Keep inquiry_clock_start = complaint_receipt (Equator policy); show a second informational date from inquiry commencement; never auto-close or penalise on breach.. Recheck: On counsel opinion; before POSH module GA. |

Equator policy: avoid these words in any entity, programme or academy name unless counsel confirms. | Residual: Exact rule and clause.. Gate: No 'National/Union/Central/Bharat' in names. II (rr.36-37 and solicitation/commission rules) permit non-cash platform credits as referral rewards to advocates. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | r.36 bars solicitation 'directly or indirectly'; a referral reward given to an advocate for bringing users or matters risks being treated as indirect solicitation or a reward for procuring work, and BCI's 2024 directive shows active enforcement against platform-mediated solicitation. | J-108 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P12_Mobile_Apps_Parity_and_Release.md:585: Whether a procedural dial-out to a FIN borrower for a hearing is a 'recovery-related call' (NN-12). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Undefined term (para 100AA). Para 100Y(4) (08:00–19:00) binds the NBFC's employees and recovery agents from 1 Jan 2027. Before that, RBC Directions 2025 para 100 already applies the same window to recovery calls on overdue loans, and para 91(2) applies 09:00–18:00 to microfinance. A hearing call in a slot the borrower chose is the weakest case for coverage, and 100Y(4) allows contact at the borrower's express request anyway. The conservative treatment is lawful under either reading. Evidence: [RBI NBFC RBC Third Amendment Directions 2026, RBI/2026-27/230](https://rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=13672); [NBFC RBC Directions 2025 text, RBI/DOR/2025-26/362 (TaxGuru reproducti](https://taxguru.in/?p=1015048) | Residual: Counsel opinion on hearing calls by a neutral institution.. Gate: Block outside the window unless the borrower selected the slot (current design). Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J-109 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | phases/P13_Public_Website_CMS_SEO_Growth.md:486: Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules 2020 apply to Equator's paid online services (own-service sales on its website/app). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Research note (not verified against the Gazette today). The Consumer Protection Act 2019 defines e-commerce as buying or selling goods or services, including digital products, over a digital network. The E-Commerce Rules apply to all goods and services sold that way and to all e-commerce models. Selling Equator's own ODR services through its own site arguably makes it an inventory-model e-commerce entity. Recommended default (Equator policy): treat the Rules as applying. Comply with r.4: grievance officer published, 48-hour acknowledgement, one-month redress, explicit consent with no pre-ticked boxes, and price breakup. Appendix O already does this. Evidence: [Department of Consumer Affairs — Consumer Protection Acts & Rules page](https://consumeraffairs.nic.in/acts-and-rules/consumer-protection) | Residual: Whether a professional/institutional dispute-resolution service is a 'service' sold via e-commerce or excluded professional service; Chambers marketplace characterisation (Q-O1). Rule text not opened today.. Gate: Comply by default (Appendix O §6.3 controls ON).. Recheck: On counsel opinion; any amendment to E-Commerce Rules. |

CGST Rules r.34(2) uses the rate under GAAP, and Ind AS 21 allows a spot or approximate rate applied consistently. Acceptance is an audit judgement, not law. Recommended option: document the policy (FBIL USDINR at about 13:00 IST ÷ 3.6725, stored with source and timestamp), ask the statutory auditor for written sign-off, and keep a switch to the AD-bank TT rate. | Residual: Auditor sign-off; no primary source fetched for the peg (well known). WebSearch budget for this run was exhausted part-way (shared 200-call limit); no further queries possible on 22 Oct 2026.. Gate: AED invoices use the derived rate with an audit trail; switch to AD-bank TT if the auditor declines. Recheck: Before first AED invoice. |

| J-132 | Regulatory watch → Company Secretary | appendices/B_Fee_Engine_and_Rate_Cards.md:860: Appendix B: under Master Circular /195 only the initiating party deposits the arbitration fees within 7 days, and 'both sides pay' is a 23 Jul 2026 draft change. | **corrected** (**closed**) | This is wrong. Master Circular /195 para 28(c) already makes arbitration fees payable at initiation 'by the initiator ... and by the person against whom the arbitration has been initiated', deposited within 7 days. If the respondent defaults, the initiator may pay and recover the amount in the arbitration, and the successful party is refunded subject to the award. The 23 Jul 2026 draft keeps both-sides payment but routes it to each party's MII. The late-fee and 40:30:30 statements are correct.
