| J-144 | Regulatory watch → Company Secretary | appendices/G_Legal_Rules_Register.md:300: LR-TEL-010: regs 21A (AI/ML UCC_Detect) and 25 (complaint thresholds, penalties) of the Third Amendment are IN_FORCE from 18 Sep 2026. | **corrected** (**closed**) | Wrong as stated. Nothing commenced on 18 Sep 2026. Reg 1(3) brings the regulations into force 30 days after Gazette publication. Reg 21A falls in that general tier, so it applies from about 18 Oct 2026 (or 28 Oct 2026 if counted from the e-Gazette upload). The Annexure items commence after 90 days, about 17 or 27 Dec 2026: amending reg 11 (reg 25 complaint-data check against AI-flagged senders, and the proviso in reg 25(4)(d)(ii)) and amending reg 9(a) (appeals). The '3 complaints + AI flag in 10 days' trigger therefore applies from the 90-day date. The '5 flagged CLIs in 10 days' trigger (reg 21A) applies from the 30-day date. Monitoring controls should stay ON now. Evidence: [Gazette copy (Ricago-hosted)](https://www.ricago.com/assets/front/base/file/file_management/7707.pdf); [PIB — 18 Sep 2026 release](https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleseDetailm.aspx?PRID=2311989&lang=1&reg=3) | Residual: Exact mapping of reg 25 penalty tiers to the 30- vs 90-day group needs the English text; publication-date ambiguity.. Gate: None — per-CLI hygiene controls (D7) stay ON from go-live.. Recheck: 17 Dec 2026. |

The Repealing and Amending Act, 2025 (Act No. 37 of 2025, dated 20 Dec 2025), Second Schedule, entry for the Indian Succession Act 1925 (Act 39 of 1925), omits s.213, removes ', 213' from s.3(1) and omits clause (b) of s.370(2). Law-firm notes give 20 Dec 2025 as the date of Presidential assent. Probate stays available, and some will matters (e.g. third-party reliance, banks) may still in practice need it. Evidence: [Repealing and Amending Act, 2025 (Act 37 of 2025), India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22046/1/a2025-37.pdf); [SCC Online blog, 29 Aug 2026 — s.213 omission](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2026/08/29/section-213-succession-act-omission-2025-probate/); [JSA Prism, Feb 2026](https://www.jsalaw.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/02/JSA-Prism-Corporate-Section-213.February-2026.Final_.pdf) | Residual: The Gazette Extraordinary page reference was not opened; India Code is the authoritative compilation. Whether the omission applies to pending proceedings is not addressed here.. Gate: None for the exclusion; copy should say 'probate no longer compulsory, may still be sought'.. Recheck: None. |
| J2-012 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:301: The SHe-Box (Sexual Harassment electronic Box) portal is the current government online complaint channel; URL and status needed. | **verified** (**closed**) | MoWCD relaunched SHe-Box as a centralised portal on 29 Aug 2024 at https://shebox.wcd.gov.in/. Complaints from women in any sector are forwarded to the relevant Internal or Local Committee and can be tracked. The portal also holds IC/LC details, annual reports and nodal officers. A PIB background document of Feb 2026 describes it as operational. It does not replace a written complaint to the IC/LC under POSH s.9. Evidence: [PIB background document (Feb 2026) referencing shebox.wcd.gov.in](https://static.pib.gov.in/WriteReadData/specificdocs/documents/2026/feb/doc2026212787301.pdf); [News on AIR — Government launches SHe-Box portal](https://www.newsonair.gov.in/government-launches-she-box-portal-to-make-workplaces-safer-for-women/) | Residual: The link has not been tested from production. Check that the URL resolves before launch.. Gate: Link-check job on the signpost URL; if it fails, show 'SHe-Box (Ministry of Women and Child Development)' text without the link.. Recheck: Quarterly link check. |
| J2-013 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:306: EXC-27 needs a per-State table of rent-control laws and fora (Rent Controller/Rent Authority) for the 'State law' column. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Seed table for launch States. Delhi: Delhi Rent Control Act 1958, Rent Controller. Uttar Pradesh: UP Regulation of Urban Premises Tenancy Act 2021 (UP Act 16 of 2021; Rent Authority/Rent Tribunal). On about 21 Aug 2026 the Allahabad HC (Inder Bhushan Sawhney v Kanchan Kumari Jain, 2026:AHC:177234-DB) struck down ss.8-10, the Rent Authority eviction powers and parts of ss.38/42, and held that the 1972 Act revives 'to the extent required'. Rajasthan: Rajasthan Rent Control Act 2001 (Rent Tribunal). MTA-type States: AP, Assam, TN, UP (RS reply 25 Jul 2022). Evidence: [UP Regulation of Urban Premises Tenancy Act 2021 (UP Act 16 of 2021), ](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/21157/1/english_16_of_2021.pdf); [LiveLaw — Allahabad HC strikes down key provisions of UP Tenancy Act 2](https://www.livelaw.in/high-court/allahabad-high-court/allahabad-high-court-key-provisions-up-tenancy-act-1972-law-revives-547147); [Verdictum — Inder Bhushan Sawhney, 2026:AHC:177234-DB](https://www.verdictum.in/allahabad-high-court/inder-bhushandead-and-2-others-neutral-citation-2026ahc177234-db-1620611) | Residual: The UP forum position is unsettled after the August 2026 judgment, and a Supreme Court appeal is likely. The Rajasthan 2001 Act and Delhi 1958 Act texts were not re-opened. Other States are not compiled.. Gate: EXC-27 copy for a State shows 'Rent Controller / Rent Authority under State law — check with a lawyer' until the State row is approved. For UP, show both Rent Authority and Small Causes/civil court with a 'law in flux' note.. Recheck: Monthly for UP (SC appeal); 22 Jan 2027 for others. |
| J2-014 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:308: The Waqf (Amendment) Act 2025 amended Waqf Act 1995 s.83; commencement and effect needed. | **verified** (**closed**) | The amendment came into force on 8 Apr 2025, after assent on 5 Apr 2025. It renames the 1995 Act the Unified Waqf Management, Empowerment, Efficiency and Development Act, 1995. Kerala HC (W.P.(C) 47612/2025, 22 Jan 2026) confirms the amended s.83: a new proviso to s.83(2) allows a direct High Court appeal where no Tribunal is functioning, and s.83(9) now gives an appeal to the High Court within 90 days of a Tribunal order (the earlier finality is gone). Tribunal exclusivity remains. Constitutional challenges are pending in the Supreme Court. Evidence: [Kerala HC, Eriyad Palli Jama-ath v State, W.P.(C) 47612/2025, 22 Jan 2](https://www.livelaw.in/pdf_upload/2026/05/15/waqf-674163.pdf); [Wikipedia — Waqf (Amendment) Act, 2025 (assent and commencement dates;](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waqf_(Amendment)_Act,_2025); [Taxmann — Waqf dispute resolution](https://www.taxmann.com/post/blog/waqf-dispute-resolution) | Residual: The Gazette commencement notification number was not seen. The outcome of the SC challenge (In re Waqf (Amendment) Act 2025) is pending; the exclusion is unaffected either way.. Gate: None for the exclusion. Recheck: On SC final judgment. |
| J2-015 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:317: Revenue-court partition provisions for launch States: UP Revenue Code 2006 s.116; Rajasthan Tenancy Act 1955 s.53; Delhi equivalent. | **provisional** (open) | Headings confirmed. UP Revenue Code 2006 s.116 is 'Suit for division of holding' (UP Government text). Rajasthan Tenancy Act 1955 s.53 is 'Division of holding' (India Code heading; body not displayed). Delhi Land Reforms Act 1954 s.55(1) reads 'A Bhumidhar may sue for partition of his holding', and Schedule I item 11 sends the suit to the Revenue Assistant (judgment text). The table is correct for UP and Rajasthan; add Delhi (DLR Act s.55, with the s.185 civil-court bar). Evidence: [UP Revenue Code 2006 (UP Govt, vaad.up.nic.in)](https://vaad.up.nic.in/pdf/Revenue_code_2006_englist_hindi_updated.pdf); [Rajasthan Tenancy Act 1955 s.53 (lawgist, India Code heading)](https://lawgist.in/states/rajasthan/rajasthan-tenancy-act-1955/53); [Indian Kanoon — judgment quoting DLR Act s.55 and Schedule I](https://future.indiankanoon.org/doc/1867043) | Residual: The full body of Rajasthan s.53 and the forum schedules were not read. Haryana and Maharashtra (MLRC 1966 s.85) are not done.. Gate: Explorer shows the generic 'Tehsildar / revenue court under State law' unless the State row is approved.. Recheck: 22 Jan 2027. |

| J2-017 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:486: A settlement signed before the conciliation officer under IR Code s.53 is binding; the binding-effect section is needed (Q-C3). | **verified** (**closed**) | Confirmed on India Code. IR Code 2020 s.53(3): where a settlement is reached in conciliation, the officer sends a report with the memorandum signed by the parties to the appropriate Government. s.57(3): a settlement reached in the course of conciliation binds all parties to the dispute, summoned parties, heirs/successors/assigns, and all workers of the establishment, including future ones. s.58(1)-(2): it operates from the agreed date (else date of signing), for the agreed period (else 6 months), and then until 60 days after a termination notice. Evidence: [Industrial Relations Code 2020 (Act 35 of 2020), India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22040/1/aa202035.pdf) | Residual: None. Gate: None. Recheck: On IR Code amendment. |
| J2-018 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:806: Telecommunications Act 2023 repeals the Indian Telegraph Act 1885; commencement of the repeal and any replacement dispute provision needed. | **provisional** (open) | A DoT notification dated 21 Jun 2024 brought ss.1, 2, 10-30, 42-44, 46, 47, 50-58, 61 and 62 into force on 26 Jun 2024. s.61 (repeal and savings) is among them, so Telegraph Act s.7B arbitration no longer applies to new disputes. Replacement: s.28(3) requires each authorised entity to run an online mechanism for registering and redressing user grievances 'as may be prescribed'. The adjudication chapter is not in the list. Vodafone Idea v Ajay Kumar Agarwal still supports consumer-forum access. Evidence: [AZB & Partners — staggered enforcement of the Telecommunications Act 2](https://azbpartners.com/bank/update-staggered-enforcement-of-the-telecommunications-act-2023); [JSA — Partial notification of the Telecommunications Act 2023](https://www.jsalaw.com/?p=51229); [Telecommunications Act 2023 s.28 text (LawX)](https://lawx.in/acts/section/123/1100/9461) | Residual: The Gazette S.O. number was not seen. Whether rules under s.28(3) have been notified, and later commencement notifications, were not checked.. Gate: Copy: 'The Telegraph Act has been repealed (from 26 Jun 2024); you can still approach the Consumer Commission.' No reliance on s.7B.. Recheck: 22 Jan 2027 (DoT notifications page). |
| J2-019 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:820: Whether coaching institutes and edtech providers render 'services' under CPA 2019 (consumer status of education is contested). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | The question is unresolved at Supreme Court level. Leave was granted on 29 Oct 2021 in Rajendra Kumar Gupta v Dr Virendra Swarup Public School (SLP(C) 16591/2021) on whether education is a 'service', tagged with Manu Solanki v Vinayaka Mission University. No final judgment was found. For coaching, CCPA has issued Guidelines for Prevention of Misleading Advertisement in Coaching Sector, in effect 13 Nov 2024. This shows CCPA treats coaching providers as within CPA 2019 regulation. Consumer fora generally entertain coaching/edtech fee-refund complaints, while statutory universities/schools remain contested. Evidence: [LiveLaw — SC grants leave on whether education is a service (order 29 ](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/education-service-consumer-protection-act-supreme-court-leave-184872); [Fox Mandal — CCPA coaching guidelines in effect from 13 Nov 2024](https://foxmandal.in/News/ccpa-issues-guidelines-for-prevention-of-misleading-advertisement-in-coaching-sector-2024/); [Bar & Bench — CCPA and coaching institutions](https://www.barandbench.com/columns/ccpa-and-coaching-institutions-two-sides-of-a-noble-endeavour) | Residual: The Manu Solanki reference is pending. No NCDRC 2025-26 ruling specific to edtech was retrieved.. Gate: Equator policy: for coaching/edtech B2C, show the Consumer Commission as an available forum ('may be available'); for universities/schools, show SGRC/Ombudsperson first and say consumer-forum status is unsettled.. Recheck: On SC judgment in Manu Solanki; 22 Jan 2027. |
| J2-020 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:834: After the 2023 amendment, the Multi-State Co-operative Societies Act 2002 provides for a Co-operative Ombudsman; section number needed. | **verified** (**closed**) | Confirmed. The Co-operative Ombudsman sits under MSCS Act 2002 s.85A, inserted by the MSCS (Amendment) Act 2023. Members complain on Form VI under MSCS Rules 2002 r.30H(2) by post or email (mscs-ombudsman@gov.in). The Ombudsman was appointed by Gazette notification dated 5 Mar 2024 (Central Registrar of Co-operative Societies site). It covers members' deposits, equitable benefits and individual rights in multi-State societies only. State societies use State law fora. Evidence: [Central Registrar of Co-operative Societies — Cooperative Ombudsman](https://crcs.gov.in/ombudsman); [Gazette — MSCS (Amendment) Act 2023](https://egazette.gov.in/WriteReadData/2023/247816.pdf) | Residual: The Gazette PDF was not opened to read s.85A's full text. Check the time limits in r.30H before displaying any.. Gate: None; add `coop_ombudsman` signpost for multi-State societies.. Recheck: Annual. |
| J2-021 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:842: Hit-and-run compensation: current amounts and process via the claims enquiry officer. | **verified** (**closed**) | Compensation to Victims of Hit and Run Motor Accidents Scheme 2022 (G.S.R. 163(E), Feb 2022; accidents on or after 1 Apr 2022): ₹2,00,000 for death and ₹50,000 for grievous hurt, less any s.162 cashless-treatment amount. The claimant files Form I and a Form IV refund undertaking with the Claims Enquiry Officer (SDO/Tehsildar). The CEO reports in Form II to the Claims Settlement Commissioner (DM/Collector) within 1 month. Sanction (Form III) follows within 15 days, and GI Council pays within 15 days. It applies only where the vehicle is untraced. Evidence: [General Insurance Council — Hit and Run scheme note](https://www.gicouncil.in/media/4276/hit-and-run-1.pdf); [Vikaspedia — Compensation to Victims of Hit and Run Motor Accidents Sc](https://en.vikaspedia.in/viewcontent/social-welfare/social-awareness/schemes/compensation-to-victims-of-hit-and-run-motor-accidents-scheme-2022) | Residual: The GIC note prints the notification date inconsistently (2022/2023); the scheme is 2022. The Gazette text was not opened.. Gate: None. Recheck: Annual (amounts may be revised). |
| J2-022 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:848: Under RB-IOS 2026, whether a non-advocate authorised representative may file a complaint. | **verified** (**closed**) | Yes. The RBI's FAQ on RB-IOS 2026 says 'A complaint may be filed through an authorised representative of the complainant' and that 'the authorised representative cannot be an advocate unless the advocate himself/herself is the aggrieved person'. So a non-advocate authorised representative may file, and an advocate may not unless personally aggrieved. Complaints received before 1 Jul 2026 stay under RB-IOS 2021. Equator can still decline to act as representative (product choice). Evidence: [RBI — FAQs on RB-IOS 2026](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/FAQDisplay.aspx?Id=153) | Residual: The scheme clause number was not read from the Master Direction text.. Gate: Keep Equator out of the representative role (as in the pack); the complaint-ready pack may say a family member or other non-advocate may file for the complainant.. Recheck: On RB-IOS amendment. |

| J2-025 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/K_Dispute_Explorer_v2_and_Dispute_Type_Framework.md:876: Department of Expenditure (PPD) OM dated 3 Jun 2024 on arbitration and mediation in domestic public procurement; file number needed. | **verified** (**closed**) | Confirmed from a copy of the OM circulated by the Arunachal Pradesh Government: No. F.1/2/2024-PPD dated 03.06.2024, Department of Expenditure, Procurement Policy Division, Ministry of Finance. As a norm, arbitration is limited to disputes valued below ₹10 crore (dispute value, not contract value). Higher-value arbitration clauses need recorded reasons and approval by the MD (CPSEs/PSBs/FIs) or the Secretary or a delegated officer not below Joint Secretary (Ministries). Evidence: [DoE OM No. F.1/2/2024-PPD dated 03.06.2024 (copy on arunachalpradesh.g](https://arunachalpradesh.gov.in/images/notice/Adobe Scan 01 Jul 2024.pdf); [Baker McKenzie InsightPlus — new guidelines](https://insightplus.bakermckenzie.com/bm/dispute-resolution/to-arbitrate-or-not-new-guidelines-for-arbitration-and-mediation-in-contracts-for-domestic-public-procurement) | Residual: Check that no later OM has superseded it (doe.gov.in not opened).. | J2-027 | Legal counsel → CTO | appendices/L_Document_Management_Court_Formats_TypeCopy_Translation.md:464: Supreme Court e-filing digital parameters (file size, PDF/A, bookmarks) for the SC_EFILING profile. | **vendor confirmation** (open) | Not confirmed. The SC E-Filing Manual (Practice Directions, Phase-II) on main.sci.nic.in requires PDF uploads, annexures as separate PDFs that are 'legible', and digitally signed e-vakalatnamas. It does not state a maximum file size, PDF/A level, OCR or bookmark rule. efiling.sci.gov.in links a PDF User Manual and Handbook that are not readable without login/navigation. Keep the profile `unverified`. The 20 MB split default is an Equator policy, not an SC rule. Evidence: [Supreme Court E-Filing Manual (main.sci.nic.in FAQ PDF)](https://main.sci.nic.in/php/FAQ/5_6246991526434439183.pdf); [SC e-filing portal](https://efiling.sci.gov.in) | Residual: The SC e-filing 3.0 handbook was not read. Recheck: After an AOR test upload; 22 Jan 2027. |

| J2-029 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/L_Document_Management_Court_Formats_TypeCopy_Translation.md:469: The NCLT/NCLAT format profile seeds SC values (1.5 spacing) pending the NCLT Rules 2016 format rule and portal limits. | **corrected** (**closed**) | The seed is wrong on spacing and margins. NCLAT Rules 2016 r.19(1): appeals 'fairly and legibly type-written or printed in double spacing on one side of standard paper', inner (top) margin about 4 cm, right 2.5 cm, left 5 cm, paginated and indexed (the NCLAT checklist repeats this). NCLT Rules 2016 r.158 (orders): A-4, double space, one side, left 5 cm, right 2.5 cm. Set the NCLT/NCLAT profile to A4, 2.0 spacing, margins top 40 / left 50 / right 25 mm. Evidence: [NCLAT Rules 2016 (Casemine text, r.19(1), r.96(1))](https://www.casemine.com/act/in/5f5f11829fca195c05cce4cb); [NCLAT checklist for filing appeals (20.09.2017)](https://nclat.gov.in/sites/default/files/2022-11/checklist_for_filing_appeals_NCLAT_20.09.2017.pdf); [NCLT Rules 2016 r.158 (TaxManagementIndia)](https://www.taxmanagementindia.com/visitor/detail_act.asp?ID=21923) | Residual: The NCLT pleading-format rule (believed to mirror NCLAT r.19) and the efiling.nclt.gov.in size limit were not read; search budget exhausted.. Gate: `NCLT` profile stays `unverified` but uses the corrected seed values; split at 20 MB pending the portal limit.. Recheck: 22 Jan 2027. |

| J2-031 † | Legal counsel → Company Secretary | appendices/L_Document_Management_Court_Formats_TypeCopy_Translation.md:498: AI-disclosure stamp text aligned to SC Draft AI Regulations 2026 Reg 43(3). | **watch** (open) | Primary text read: Supreme Court notice of 03.06.2026 on the draft Regulations for Use of AI in Courts, 2026 (comments by 20.06.2026). Draft Reg 43(3): AI use in preparing or submitting a document, pleading or evidence must be disclosed to the court at submission by a signed declaration in the Annexure I format. Reg 43(4) lets the court demand details; Reg 43(6) makes the submitter fully responsible. Commencement is by CJI/HC notification (Reg 1(2)). The stamp is compatible, but a signed declaration will also be needed. Evidence: [Supreme Court of India — notice and Draft Regulations for Use of AI in](https://cdnbbsr.s3waas.gov.in/s3ec0490f1f4972d133619a60c30f3559e/uploads/2026/06/2026060342.pdf); [The Leaflet — explainer](https://theleaflet.in/law-and-technology/explained-the-supreme-court-of-indias-draft-regulations-for-use-of-artificial-intelligence-in-courts-2026) | Residual: Annexure I text not extracted. Final notification not seen (search budget exhausted).. Gate: Keep the stamp; add a signed 'AI-use declaration' export modelled on draft Annexure I, OFF until Reg 43 is notified for the forum.. Recheck: Monthly; trigger = CJI notification under Reg 1(2)(a). |

| J2-055 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:48: Route by which a non-EU controller (Equator) may process GDPR Art. 10 criminal-offence data (e.g., NI Act s.138 proceedings status) for EU-party matters. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Under Art. 10, criminal-conviction and offence data based on Art. 6(1) may be processed only under the control of official authority, or when authorised by Union or Member State law with appropriate safeguards. | J2-061 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:56: Current version/date of IRDAI Information and Cyber Security Guidelines (and the RBI cyber/IT framework of 31 Jul 2026) for flow-down to insurer tenants. | **provisional** (open) | Best answer [K, not re-verified today]: IRDAI Information and Cyber Security Guidelines, 2023 (24 Apr 2023), which require incident reporting to CERT-In within 6 hours and cover outsourcing and audit of third parties. Equator's 6-hour CERT-In capability and a ≤ 24-hour tenant notice already meet the strictest timeline. | Residual: IRDAI version/date; RBI 31 Jul 2026 framework citation.. | J2-062 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:127: PC-36: anonymised data is out of DPDP scope only if irreversible; DPDP s.3(c)(ii) excludes data made publicly available by the Data Principal. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | The DPDP Act applies to 'personal data', meaning data about an individual identifiable by or in relation to it (s.2(t)). It has no anonymisation definition or standard, and the DPDP Rules 2025 add none. s.3(c)(ii) excludes data made publicly available by the Data Principal or under a legal duty. Publishing an award does not make it 'made available by the Data Principal'. Equator policy: treat published outcomes as personal data unless re-identification testing shows identification is not reasonably likely, and keep consent of all parties (NN-26). Evidence: [DPDP Act 2023 (Gazette)](https://egazette.gov.in/WriteReadData/2023/248045.pdf) | Residual: Threshold for 'identifiable'; no Board guidance yet.. Gate: Publication requires all-party consent + redaction profile + re-identification test (unchanged).. Recheck: On any MeitY/Board anonymisation guidance. |
| J2-063 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:235: EDPB Guidelines 9/2022 v2: a non-EU controller gets no one-stop-shop and must notify each SA where affected data subjects reside. | **verified** (**closed**) | Confirmed. EDPB Guidelines 9/2022 on personal data breach notification, Version 2.0, adopted 28 Mar 2023, para 73: 'the mere presence of a representative in a Member State does not trigger the one-stop-shop system'. The breach must be notified to every supervisory authority in whose Member State affected data subjects reside. The controller remains responsible, and the Art. 27 representative may help. Evidence: [EDPB Guidelines 9/2022 v2.0 (PDF)](https://www.edpb.europa.eu/system/files/2023-04/edpb_guidelines_202209_personal_data_breach_notification_v2.0_en.pdf) | Residual: None.. Gate: None. Recheck: On EDPB update. |
| J2-064 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:242: California SB 446 (2025) introduced fixed deadlines in Civ. Code §1798.82: 30 days to notify residents and 15 days to submit the sample notice to the AG. | **provisional** (open) | Not verified today: leginfo.legislature.ca.gov returned errors, and the AG breach page does not state day-limits. The best available understanding (unverified) is that SB 446 sets 30 calendar days from discovery for resident notice and 15 days after that for the AG sample notice (where more than 500 residents are affected), effective 1 Jan 2026. CCPA/California law does not reach Equator at launch, so keep breach.us_ca.days = 30 as a conservative configuration. Evidence: [California AG — Data breach reporting](https://oag.ca.gov/privacy/databreach/reporting) | Residual: Statute text and effective date.. Gate: US-CA breach module dormant unless California residents' data held; config 30 days.. Recheck: Before any US-CA processing. |
| J2-065 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:268: EDPB Guidelines 05/2021 (final): direct collection from an EU data subject by a non-EU controller is not a Chapter V transfer. | **verified** (**closed**) | Confirmed. EDPB Guidelines 05/2021 on the interplay between the application of Article 3 and the provisions on international transfers under Chapter V GDPR, Version 2.0, adopted 14 Feb 2023 after consultation. There are three cumulative criteria for a 'transfer', and Example 1 holds that a data subject in the EU disclosing data directly to a non-EU controller is not a transfer, because no exporter exists. Art. 3(2) compliance then applies directly. Evidence: [EDPB Guidelines 05/2021 v2.0 (PDF)](https://www.edpb.europa.eu/system/files/2023-02/edpb_guidelines_05-2021_interplay_between_the_application_of_art3-chapter_v_of_the_gdpr_v2_en_0.pdf) | Residual: None.. Gate: None. Recheck: On EDPB update. |

| J2-068 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:939: Appendix Q §10 item 1 (Q-R2): commencement split of DPDP s.44 under G.S.R. 843(E); does s.43A survive to 13 May 2027? | **verified** (**closed**) | Yes. G.S.R. 843(E) clause (a) brings s.44(1) (TRAI Act) and s.44(3) (RTI Act s.8(1)(j)) into force on 13 Nov 2025; clause (c) brings s.44(2) (omission of IT Act s.43A, amendment of s.81 proviso, omission of s.87(2)(ob)) into force eighteen months after publication, i.e. 13 May 2027. Until then s.43A and the SPDI Rules 2011 apply. Same finding as J2-054; LR-DPDP-001 corrected there. Evidence: [MeitY — Gazette G.S.R. 843(E), 13 Nov 2025](https://www.meity.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/11/c56ceae6c383460ca69577428d36828b.pdf); [DPDP Act s.44 text (dpdpa.com reproduction)](https://www.dpdpa.com/dpdpa2023/chapter-9/section44.html) | Residual: None on the commencement split; date arithmetic computed from publication.. Gate: None. Recheck: 13 May 2027 (flip LR-ITA-001 to REPEALED). |
| J2-069 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:940: SPDI Rules r.8: ISO/IEC 27001:2022 certification satisfies the 'IS/ISO/IEC 27001' deemed-compliance standard until s.43A is omitted (13 May 2027). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | SPDI r.8(2) names 'the international Standard IS/ISO/IEC 27001 on Information Technology – Security Techniques – Information Security Management System – Requirements' without an edition [K], so the current 2022 edition is the natural reading. r.8(4) requires an audit at least once a year by a government-approved auditor, or on significant upgrade. s.43A and the SPDI Rules fall away on 13 May 2027 (G.S.R. 843(E) cl.(c), verified). Exposure is short. Recommend accepting 27001:2022 plus annual audit as the interim position. Evidence: [MeitY — Gazette G.S.R. 843(E) (s.44(2) from 13 May 2027)](https://www.meity.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/11/c56ceae6c383460ca69577428d36828b.pdf) | Residual: SPDI r.8 text not re-opened today; the pre-certification gap until ISO certificate (Stage 2 ≤ L+150 d).. Gate: Documented ISMS + annual audit from launch.. Recheck: 13 May 2027 (retire). |
| J2-070 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:941: GDPR Art. 3(2) trigger for INT marketing (EUR pricing, EU-language pages); 'occasional' processing exemption under Art. 27(2)(a); large-scale Art. 9 data of EU-resident diaspora parties. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | This is interpretation for counsel. Under EDPB Guidelines 3/2018 on territorial scope [K], indicators of 'offering' include EU currency, EU languages and EU-targeted marketing. A single ad hoc matter is not 'targeting', but systematic EUR pricing would be. The Art. 27(2)(a) exemption requires occasional processing with no large-scale Art. 9/10 data. FAM religion data, if systematic, risks losing it. Recommended default (A-05 already): no EU targeting at launch; appoint an Art. 27 representative before turning on any EU-targeted page or EUR price. Evidence: [EDPB Guidelines 05/2021 v2.0 (Art. 3 interplay)](https://www.edpb.europa.eu/system/files/2023-02/edpb_guidelines_05-2021_interplay_between_the_application_of_art3-chapter_v_of_the_gdpr_v2_en_0.pdf) | Residual: Threshold facts; Art. 27 exemption.. Gate: No EUR pricing/EU-language marketing until Art. 27 representative appointed.. Recheck: Before EU marketing. |
| J2-071 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:942: EU AI Act: neutral-assist features on EU-party matters make Equator a provider of an Annex III 8(a) system, or fall within Art. 6(3); Art. 27 FRIA for a private ADR body. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Annex III point 8(a) text checked. It covers AI intended to assist a judicial authority in researching and interpreting facts and law and applying the law to facts. It also covers AI 'used in a similar way in alternative dispute resolution', so neutral-assist on ADR matters is in scope. Art. 6(3) can exclude narrow procedural or preparatory tasks if documented. The FRIA under Art. 27 binds deployers that are public bodies or private entities providing public services, plus certain Annex III 5 deployers; whether a private ADR institution qualifies is unclear. The application date as amended by the Digital Omnibus was not verified today. Default (LR-INT-020): clerical, translation and summarisation only on EU-party matters. Evidence: [EU AI Act Annex III (artificialintelligenceact.eu)](https://artificialintelligenceact.eu/annex/3/) | Residual: Omnibus application date; Art. 6(3) documentation; FRIA scope.. Gate: Neutral-assist AI OFF for EU-party matters.. Recheck: Before any EU-party INT matter; 2 Dec 2027. |

| J2-078 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:949: Appendix Q §10 item 11: anonymisation threshold for publication to fall outside DPDP. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Same as J2-062. DPDP has no anonymisation standard, and data stays 'personal' while the individual is identifiable (s.2(t)). | J2-079 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:950: Appendix Q §10 item 12: 5-year KYC retention default for a non-PMLA-reporting entity. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Equator is not a PMLA reporting entity, so PMLA s.12's 5-year rule is a benchmark, not an obligation [K]. Under DPDP s.8(7) and r.8, data must be erased once the purpose ends, unless retention is needed for compliance with law. The defensible period is tied to the case lifecycle: keep identity evidence with the case file (8 years, or 13 years for the outcome_instrument class, Q-R4), because it proves who was a party to an enforceable award. Recommend counsel approve 'KYC evidence retained with the case file's retention class' instead of a free-standing 5 years. Evidence: [MeitY — Gazette G.S.R. 846(E) DPDP Rules 2025](https://www.meity.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/11/53450e6e5dc0bfa85ebd78686cadad39.pdf) | Residual: Counsel approval of retention linkage.. Gate: Retention default stays 5 years after relationship end until counsel decides (no early deletion).. Recheck: Before 13 May 2027. |
| J2-080 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/R_Case_Status_Publication_Execution_Registration.md:441: Limitation for executing a domestic arbitral award is 12 years under Limitation Act Art. 136 (not 3 years under Art. 137). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Research note (not re-searched; web-search budget exhausted). Government of India v Vedanta Ltd (2020) 10 SCC 1, already cited in the pack for X7, held that a foreign-award enforcement petition is governed by Art. 137 (3 years) because a foreign award is not a decree until the court is satisfied under s.49. The contrast is that a domestic award is enforceable 'as if it were a decree' under s.36(1), which supports Art. 136 (12 years from when it becomes enforceable). High Courts have generally applied Art. 136. | Residual: Paragraph-level confirmation of Vedanta and any contrary HC view.. Gate: Keep the 12-year clock (informational) running from the end of the s.34(3) window, plus an Equator-policy prompt at 3 years: 'execute without delay'. Never tell a user an execution is time-barred.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J2-081 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/R_Case_Status_Publication_Execution_Registration.md:443: Limitation start for challenging and enforcing a s.74 conciliation settlement. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Research note (no primary source opened). s.74 gives a signed s.73 settlement the 'same status and effect' as an arbitral award on agreed terms under s.30. s.73(4) requires the conciliator to authenticate it and give a copy to each party. By analogy with s.34(3), which runs from receipt of the award, the safest start for any challenge window and the s.36 enforceability clock is each party's receipt of the authenticated copy. Whether s.34 applies to a s.74 settlement at all is debated, so it needs counsel. | Residual: Case law on s.34 challenges to s.73/s.74 settlements.. Gate: Record the per-party delivery date of the s.73(4) authenticated copy; run the X1 clocks from it; show 'confirm with counsel'.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J2-082 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/R_Case_Status_Publication_Execution_Registration.md:456: In a contractual two-tier arbitration, s.34 time runs from receipt of the final (appellate) award (Q-R6); also Centrotrade and s.29A. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Research note. Centrotrade Minerals & Metal Inc v Hindustan Copper Ltd (2017) 2 SCC 228 upheld two-tier (appellate) arbitration as valid. The appellate award is then the arbitral award for s.34/s.36, and s.34(3) logically runs from its receipt. Open points for counsel: whether a party must also challenge the first-tier award protectively, and how the s.29A time limit (12 months from completion of pleadings, domestic) applies to each tier. Conservative reading: treat each tier as a separate mandate with its own s.29A clock. | Residual: No primary text re-read this session (search budget exhausted).. Gate: Two-tier option stays behind LR-ARB-026 flag; show both tiers' s.29A clocks; challenge clock from the final award, with a 'protective challenge — ask counsel' note.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J2-083 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/R_Case_Status_Publication_Execution_Registration.md:468: Pecuniary limits of the Commercial Divisions of High Courts with ordinary original civil jurisdiction (Delhi, Bombay, Calcutta, Madras, Himachal Pradesh). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Not verified (search budget exhausted). Under CCA s.4, a Commercial Division hears commercial disputes of specified value that would otherwise be within the High Court's original jurisdiction, so the threshold equals each HC's pecuniary original-side limit, as revised by State legislation (e.g. Delhi High Court (Amendment) Act 2015, ₹2 crore). Several limits have been revised since 2015, so values must come from current Acts and notifications. | Residual: All five values.. Gate: `core.court_jurisdiction_rules` rows stay UNVERIFIED; resolver output for FR-1 reads 'Commercial Division of the High Court or the Commercial Court, depending on value — your advocate will confirm'.. Recheck: Before FR-1 goes live. |
| J2-084 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | appendices/R_Case_Status_Publication_Execution_Registration.md:471: Whether Sundaram Finance (asset-situs filing without transfer) applies to India-seated international commercial arbitration awards, whose 'court' under s.2(1)(e)(ii) is the High Court. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Research note. Sundaram Finance v Abdul Samad (2018) 3 SCC 622 held a domestic award can be executed anywhere in India where assets are, without obtaining a transfer from the s.2(1)(e) court. For ICA awards, s.2(1)(e)(ii) names the High Court, and s.10(1) CCA sends matters to the Commercial Division. Whether execution must then be filed in a High Court with ordinary original jurisdiction where assets lie, or in the High Court of the asset-situs State, needs counsel. | Residual: HC case law on ICA award execution forum.. Gate: FR-4 output: 'High Court (Commercial Division) — the correct High Court depends on where assets are; counsel to confirm'; no auto-generated forum name.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |

I Art. 48 and State equivalents). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | s.1A defines a power-of-attorney as any instrument empowering a person 'to act for and in the name of' the executant; an LoA to file, attend and receive notices arguably still authorises acting in the party's name, so the safer view is that it may be a PoA. If so, IT Act s.1(4)/First Schedule excludes it from e-signature, and stamp duty under Art. 48 (or the State article) may apply. Counsel should give a State-by-State view; interim, keep S-D1. | Residual: No authority found this round; search budget exhausted.. Its reach to an institution (not the claimant) and to data the respondent supplies voluntarily is interpretive. The pack's default (no respondent verification without the respondent's own action) is conservative and should stand. | Residual: No judicial guidance on s.17(1)(a); statute text not re-opened this round.. Gate: Respondent verified only on own action. Recheck: Counsel opinion. |

| J2-107 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V1_FIN_Financial_and_Loan.md:165: No RBI rule compels a lender to report a compromise-settled loan as 'closed'; 'settled' is a commercial term, not an error. | **provisional** (open) | No RBI provision found that requires 'closed'. The NBFC RBC Directions 2025 have no settled-account paragraph, and RBI/2023-24/72 and /73 deal with timelines, alerts and rejection reasons only. The CIC format treats 'Settled' (UCRF Tag 22 code 03, with Settlement Amount) as a distinct, accurate state. 'Settled' is therefore not an error. The reverse is the risk: CICRA s.19 requires accurate and complete data, so reporting 'closed' after a short payment may be inaccurate. Evidence: [NBFC RBC Directions 2025 text, RBI/DOR/2025-26/362 (TaxGuru reproducti](https://taxguru.in/?p=1015048); [RBI/2023-24/72 CIC compensation framework](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12554); [RBI/2023-24/73 Strengthening customer service by CICs and CIs](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12553) | Residual: Bank RE-specific Directions not read; counsel to confirm the CICRA s.19 reading. MSPs are recognised by the Mediation Council of India (Mediation Act ss.38(i)–(j), 40(1)(a), 40(2)); LSAs and court-annexed centres are deemed MSPs. s.40 is not in force; MCI established by S.O. 4781(E), 27 Aug 2026. | J2-161 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:85: SC direction of 19 Nov 2025 on pre-litigation mediation for HSA s.15 disputes (PIL by Snidha Mehra): order text, case number, binding scope. | **verified** (**closed**) | Verified from the order text. The case is Snidha Mehra v Union of India, W.P.(C) 732/2020, decided 19 Nov 2025 by Nagarathna and Mahadevan JJ. The Court left the validity of s.15(1)(b) open. The direction applies where a Hindu woman dies intestate, her parents or the heirs of her father or mother claim under s.15(1)(c)–(e), and s.15(2) does not apply. Parties must mediate before any suit, at a Mediation Centre or a State/District/Taluk Legal Services Authority. A settlement there 'must be treated as a decree'. Private ODR is not mentioned. Evidence: [Snidha Mehra v Union of India, W.P.(C) 732/2020, SC order 19 Nov 2025 ](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/180285266/); [LiveLaw report on Snidha Mehra order](https://www.livelaw.in/top-stories/supreme-court-urges-hindu-women-to-make-wills-mandates-pre-litigation-mediation-wives-dying-intestate-succession-act-310471) | Residual: Neutral citation not seen; whether an Equator (private) mediation satisfies the direction — the order names only court-annexed Mediation Centres and LSAs.. Gate: fam.s15_prelit stays OFF; intake for this fact pattern signposts the Mediation Centre/LSA route.. Recheck: On counsel opinion (Q3 in 02 §5). |

The scope is narrower than the row label suggests. It covers claims under s.15(1)(c)–(e) by parents or the heirs of the father or mother, where s.15(2) does not apply. The prescribed forum is a Mediation Centre or a Legal Services Authority. | J2-163 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:167: One-year bar: leave to present within one year — Delhi HC Full Bench Shiksha Kumari v Santosh Kumar, 2025:DHC:11467-FB (17 Dec 2025); only a secondary report. | **verified** (**closed**) | Verified on the Delhi HC website. The case is MAT.APP.(F.C.) 111/2025, a Full Bench (Navin Chawla, Anup Jairam Bhambhani and Renu Bhatnagar JJ), decided 17 Dec 2025. The holding is about s.13B, not only s.14. The s.13B(1) one-year separation may be waived under the s.14(1) proviso. The s.13B(2) six months may be waived independently. The decree need not be deferred. Separately, Amit Kumar v Suman Beniwal is an SC order of 11 Dec 2021 (SLP(C) 20108/2021, Art. 142). Its '(2023) 17 SCC 648' citation could not be confirmed. Evidence: [Delhi HC — Shiksha Kumari v Santosh Kumar, MAT.APP.(F.C.) 111/2025, Fu](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/AJB17122025MATFC1112025_151025.txt); [Amit Kumar v Suman Beniwal, SC, 11 Dec 2021, SLP(C) 20108/2021 (Indian](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/159769574/) | Residual: Neutral citation 2025:DHC:11467-FB not seen on the judgment text; SCC cite of Amit Kumar unconfirmed. Full Bench binds Delhi courts only.. Gate: Waiver logic stays 'hard alert + lawyer review'; Delhi-only note in copy.. Recheck: 22 Jan 2027. |
| J2-164 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:169: Christian mutual consent: Divorce Act s.10A (ins. 2001), ≥ 2 years separate, 6 months; Kerala HC reading-down of s.10A(1) — citation. | **provisional** (open) | There are two Kerala HC decisions. Saumya Ann Thomas v Union of India (Ker HC, 2010) read the two-year separation in s.10A(1) as one year. Anup Disalva v Union of India, 2022 LiveLaw (Ker) 640 (DB, Muhamed Mustaque and Shoba Annamma Eapen JJ, 9 Dec 2022) then struck down the one-year minimum as unconstitutional. Both bind only in Kerala. Elsewhere the statutory two years applies. Both were read from secondary sources. Evidence: [LiveLaw — Kerala HC strikes down s.10A one-year waiting period (Anup D](https://www.livelaw.in/amp/top-stories/kerala-high-court-christian-divorce-strikes-down10a-divorce-act-1869-one-year-waiting-period-filing-divorce-petition-mutual-consent-216281); [Cornell Gender Justice — Saumya Ann Thomas v Union of India (Ker HC 20](https://www.law.cornell.edu/gender-justice/resource/saumya_ann_thomas_v_union_of_india) | Residual: Primary judgment texts and case numbers; whether an appeal to SC is pending.. Gate: Calculator uses 2 years outside Kerala; Kerala shows 'no minimum separation (Kerala HC)' with lawyer review.. Recheck: On obtaining Kerala HC judgment text. |

| J2-168 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:193: MWPSC ss.4, 9: senior citizens (Indian citizens 60+) and parents whether or not senior citizens (s.2(d)); whether a parent under 60 must be an Indian citizen. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Checked against India Code. s.2(h) defines a senior citizen as 'any person being a citizen of India' aged 60 or above. s.2(d) defines a parent as a biological, adoptive or step father or mother, with no age or citizenship test. s.4(1) lets 'a senior citizen including parent' apply, and s.4(1)(i) lets a parent apply against a non-minor child. The words 'whether or not senior citizens' are not in s.2(d). Whether a non-citizen parent under 60 qualifies is an open interpretation point. The text supports eligibility, but no authority was found. Evidence: [India Code — Maintenance and Welfare of Parents and Senior Citizens Ac](https://indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19405/1/maintenance_and_welfare_of_parents_and_senior_citizens_act%2c2007.pdf) | Residual: Citizenship of a parent under 60 (and of a non-citizen parent over 60); no case law located (search budget exhausted).. | J2-170 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:244: s.213 omitted with a savings clause for pending proceedings/accrued rights; Act number; consequential effect on the s.370 bar and on deaths before 20 Dec 2025. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Act number verified (No. 37 of 2025). The s.370 effect is verified. s.370(1) now bars a succession certificate only where s.212 requires letters of administration, and s.370(2)(b) is omitted. Section 4 is a general savings clause covering things already done, accrued rights and pending proceedings. Commentators differ on whether it saves amendments as well as repeals. SCC Online (Aug 2026) argues s.213 was procedural, so the omission also covers earlier deaths. No court ruling was found. Evidence: [India Code — The Repealing and Amending Act, 2025 (Act No. 37 of 2025)](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22046/1/A2025-37.pdf); [SCC Online blog, 29 Aug 2026 — Legal effect of omission of s.213](https://www.scconline.com/blog/post/2026/08/29/section-213-succession-act-omission-2025-probate/); [Bar & Bench — Omission of s.213: probate no longer mandatory](https://www.barandbench.com/view-point/omission-of-section-213-of-the-indian-succession-act-1925-seeking-probate-of-will-is-no-longer-mandatory) | Residual: Transitional effect on wills of testators who died before 20 Dec 2025 and on pending probate suits; whether s.4 savings applies to amendments; General Clauses Act s.6 on omissions (Fibre Boards (2015) 10 SCC 333 vs Rayala/Kolhapur Canesugar).. Gate: Copy must say nothing about deaths before 20 Dec 2025; probate workflow keeps 'probate may still be required by institutions' warning.. Recheck: On first HC/SC ruling on the s.213 omission; else 22 Jan 2027. |
| J2-171 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:246: Limitation for probate/LoA: Limitation Act Art. 137 (three years from when the right to apply accrues; continuing right) — Kunvarjeet Singh Khandpur v Kirandeep Kaur (2008) 8 SCC 463. | **verified** (**closed**) | Verified from the judgment text, which Rajasthan HC hosts as an SC judgment. The case is Civil Appeal 2464 of 2008, decided 3 Apr 2008 by Pasayat and Sathasivam JJ. Art. 137 applies to probate and letters-of-administration petitions. 'The crucial expression in the Article 137 is "right to apply"', and the right is continuing, so it does not necessarily accrue on death. The SCC page citation is widely used but was not seen on an official page. Evidence: [Kunvarjeet Singh Khandpur v Kirandeep Kaur, C.A. 2464/2008, SC 3 Apr 2](https://hcraj.nic.in/sci/2008/3-2464-2008_2008-04-03.pdf) | Residual: SCC page cite unconfirmed; use C.A. number in copy.. Gate: None; copy may use it with 'information only'.. Recheck: n/a. |

| J2-183 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V4_INS_Insurance.md:150: Whether the Insurance Ombudsman has jurisdiction over PMJJBY/PMSBY claims (group schemes administered through banks). | **provisional** (open) | Probably yes, against the insurer. Rule 3 covers 'group insurance policies', and the CIO FAQ admits complaints from a group member, or the nominee after death, where the claim is payable to the individual. PMJJBY and PMSBY claims are paid that way. Bank lapses, such as enrolment or the premium auto-debit, are not insurer or intermediary deficiencies and go to the bank's grievance route. The 2025 draft would add government schemes expressly. Equator policy: Ombudsman for insurer decisions, bank route for bank lapses. Evidence: [Insurance Ombudsman Rules 2017 r.3 (CIO consolidated)](https://www.cioins.co.in/notification/Insurance%20Ombudsman%20Rules,%202017(%20as%20amended%20till%2009.11.2023).pdf); [CIO FAQs (group policies)](https://www.cioins.co.in/Faqs); [Gazette Tracker: draft G.S.R. 864(E) (govt schemes in scope)](https://gazettetracker.com/g/CG-DL-E-25112025-267956) | Residual: No PMJJBY/PMSBY-specific Ombudsman award was located; the bank's role (master policyholder or corporate agent) was not confirmed from scheme rules.. Gate: Signposting shows the Ombudsman as 'likely available — insurer decisions only' and the bank route for bank lapses. Recheck: On notification of G.S.R. 864(E), or counsel note. |

Evidence: [Swati v Rakesh Kumar, Del HC, 16 Sep 2026 (para 29)](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/40835346/); [LiveLaw on SC interim order](https://www.livelaw.in/top-stories/no-motor-accident-claim-should-be-dismissed-as-time-barred-supreme-court-interim-order-s1663-mv-act-309095) | Residual: Final judgment.. | J2-193 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V4_INS_Insurance.md:362: V4 J-03: the Ombudsman centre is resolved by the complainant's address district from the CIO jurisdiction table; open: the rule governing territorial jurisdiction after the 2021 amendment. | **corrected** (**closed**) | Under r.14(1), the complaint goes to the Ombudsman with jurisdiction over either the branch or office of the insurer or broker complained against, or the complainant's residential address. The 2021 amendment (G.S.R. 147(E), 2 Mar 2021) only added insurance brokers. Under r.11(2), the Council for Insurance Ombudsmen fixes each centre's territory; the CIO lists 18 centres, mostly by state or district. So the residence district is a valid default, but the engine must also offer the insurer-branch centre. The 2025 draft would let the Council allot online complaints by workload. Evidence: [Insurance Ombudsman Rules 2017 r.11, r.14(1) (CIO consolidated)](https://www.cioins.co.in/notification/Insurance%20Ombudsman%20Rules,%202017(%20as%20amended%20till%2009.11.2023).pdf); [Insurance Ombudsman (Amendment) Rules 2021, G.S.R. 147(E)](https://www.cioins.co.in//notification/225587.pdf); [CIO: Offices of Ombudsman (jurisdiction table)](https://www.cioins.co.in/Ombudsman) | Residual: The CIO page shows duplicate entries with differing district lists (e.g. Lucknow, Noida); the centre_map must be versioned and refreshed from the CIO.. Gate: centre_map keyed by residence district plus an alternative 'insurer branch' selector; refresh quarterly from cioins.co.in/Ombudsman. Recheck: Quarterly, or on notification of G.S.R. 864(E). |
| J2-194 | Legal counsel → Product owner (CEO) | verticals/V4_INS_Insurance.md:378: Leap-day handling: a one-year window triggered on 29 Feb ends on 28 Feb of the next year (ins.leap_day_rule = 'feb28'). | **product decision** (open) | Recommend keeping 'feb28'. Indian computation excludes the trigger day (General Clauses Act s.9; Limitation Act s.12(1)) and counts calendar years (GCA s.3(66)). A year after 29 Feb 2028 therefore runs from 1 Mar 2028 to 28 Feb 2029. Read as a 'corresponding date', it would be 1 Mar 2029, so feb28 is the earlier, conservative reading, consistent with the binding conservative date rule. Golden tests OE-04 and OE-09 already encode this. Counsel confirmation is non-blocking. Authorities are recalled, not re-fetched. | Residual: No Indian authority on a 29 Feb trigger found; counsel may confirm.. Gate: None; feb28 is the conservative default. Recheck: None required. |
| J2-195 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V4_INS_Insurance.md:389: V4 L-04: the ins_policy_suit_bar clock is per wording and labelled 'contestable', pending the s.28 (1997) check. | **verified** (**closed**) | The 'contestable' label is right. For policies issued on or after 8 Jan 1997, a clause extinguishing the insured's rights after a fixed period is void to that extent under Contract Act s.28(b). Authority: IndusInd Bank (2016), which holds the amendment prospective, then Oriental Insurance v Sanjesh (SC, 2022) and H P Spinning Mills (Del HC DB, 13 Oct 2025). The clock may warn but must never close a route. Add a flag for policies issued before 8 Jan 1997, where Sujir Ganesh Nayak may uphold the clause. Evidence: [H P Spinning Mills v United India Insurance, Del HC DB, 13 Oct 2025](https://delhihighcourt.nic.in/app/showFileJudgment/68013102025FAOOS4262009_140932.pdf); [Union of India v IndusInd Bank, SC, 2016](https://hcraj.nic.in/sci/2016/3-9087-2016_2016-09-15.pdf) | Residual: Intimation conditions (see J-002).. Gate: Clock is AP-L warning only; no eligibility effect. Recheck: Annual case-law review. |

| J2-199 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V5_WRK_Workplace_and_HR.md:62: Whether an A&C Act s.74 conciliation settlement binds an IR Code industrial dispute and bars a Tribunal reference. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | IR Code s.2(zi): 'settlement' covers a conciliation settlement and a written agreement signed in the prescribed manner with copies to the authorised officer and conciliation officer. s.57(1): such a bipartite settlement binds the parties to it; s.57(3): conciliation settlements bind all. Central r.4: Form I, prescribed signatories, joint copy to Dy CLC (Central) and conciliation officer (r.4(5)). An A&C s.74 settlement alone does not meet s.2(zi); whether it bars a reference is untested. Evidence: [Industrial Relations Code, 2020 (Act 35 of 2020) — India Code PDF](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/22040/1/aa202035.pdf); [Industrial Relations (Central) Rules, 2026, G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026 ](https://prsindia.org/files/bills_acts/bills_parliament/2026/IR_Rules_2026.pdf) | Residual: No authority found on A&C Part III in industrial disputes.. Gate: Keep the pack rule: worker industrial disputes output an IR Code Form I settlement pack; never label as A&C s.74 award.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |

| J2-233 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V6_INT_International_Commercial.md:77: A s.73/s.74 conciliation settlement has award status in India, but its recognition abroad under the New York Convention is uncertain. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Uncertainty confirmed, and there is a second problem. The NYC applies to 'arbitral awards'; a s.74 settlement is a statutory deemed award with no arbitral tribunal, so foreign courts may refuse NYC treatment. The Singapore Convention also excludes 'settlement agreements that have been recorded and are enforceable as an arbitral award' (Art. 1(3)(b)), and a s.74 settlement arguably falls within that. A s.74 settlement may therefore lose both routes abroad. The pack's default (consent-award conversion cross-border) is right; s.74 should be India-only. Evidence: [Singapore Convention on Mediation, Art. 1(3)(b) — UNCITRAL text](https://uncitral.un.org/sites/default/files/media-documents/EN/Texts/UNCITRAL/Arbitration/mediation_convention_v1900316_eng.pdf) | Residual: No foreign case law on s.74 settlements was found (search budget exhausted).. Gate: s.74 route offered only where enforcement is expected in India; cross-border users see a consent award or an unconverted iMSA with an Art. 4 attestation.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J2-234 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V6_INT_International_Commercial.md:93: Cyprus and Saudi Arabia: whether notified as reciprocating territories under A&C s.44(b). | **provisional** (open) | Not confirmed. The Gazette search could not be run because the shared web-search budget was used up. Earlier pack research (RES_A1b Q29) also could not confirm either. The UAE is not notified under s.44(b); its 2020 notification is under CPC s.44A. Mauritius (S.O. 1881(E), 13 Jul 2015) and China/HK/Macao (S.O. 580(E), 19 Mar 2012) stand. Equator policy: treat Cyprus and Saudi Arabia seats as 'notification unconfirmed', so the seat badge is off and the non-notified acknowledgement is shown. | Residual: egazette.gov.in check for S.O. numbers under s.44(b) for Cyprus and Saudi Arabia.. Gate: `INT_SEAT_NOT_NOTIFIED` warning shown for Cyprus/Saudi Arabia seats until a Gazette S.O. is recorded.. Recheck: Next research round (egazette.gov.in, MEA/Law Ministry list). |
| J2-235 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V6_INT_International_Commercial.md:98: The one-year time bar for cargo claims is in Art. III(6) of the Schedule to the Carriage of Goods by Sea Act 2025. | **corrected** (**closed**) | India Code text of Act 19 of 2025: the time bar is in Schedule Art. III para 6(c). The carrier is discharged 'unless suit is brought within one year after delivery of the goods or the date when the goods should have been delivered', extendable by agreement after the cause of action arises. A proviso allows a further three months as the court permits. Para 6 has (a)-(d) and no 6bis. The Act has been in force since 10 Sep 2025 (S.O. 4082(E), 8 Sep 2025). Evidence: [Carriage of Goods by Sea Act 2025 (Act 19 of 2025), India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/21917/1/A2025-19.pdf) | Residual: Scope of the 3-month proviso (indemnity actions or general) needs a legal read.. Gate: None for the clock; copy 'one year after delivery — suit must be filed; extensions only by agreement'.. Recheck: None. |
| J2-236 † | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V6_INT_International_Commercial.md:514: Unreasoned awards (parties agree no reasons, A&C s.31(3)(a)) may face enforcement problems in some target countries. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Research note (no per-country survey possible; search budget exhausted). NYC Art. V lists no 'reasons' ground. The risk comes from Art. V(1)(d) (procedure not per the parties' agreement or seat law) and Art. V(2)(b) (forum public policy) where a jurisdiction treats reasons as mandatory. India permits the agreement (s.31(3)(a)). For each launch target country (UAE, Singapore, UK, US, Saudi Arabia), counsel should confirm whether an agreed unreasoned award offends local public policy. | Residual: Per-country survey.. Gate: `tier_unreasoned_award` shows a warning for all target countries; Equator policy default = reasoned award.. Recheck: Before HEAP launch. |
| J2-237 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V6_INT_International_Commercial.md:593: Whether an emergency arbitrator may grant a protective preliminary order without notice for an India seat (natural justice). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Research note. India did not adopt the 2006 Model Law Art. 17B-17C preliminary-order regime. A&C s.18 (equal treatment and full opportunity) and s.24(2)-(3) (notice and sharing of material) apply to all proceedings. Amazon v Future Retail (2022) 1 SCC 209 upheld EA orders under institutional rules as s.17 orders but did not address ex parte relief. An ex parte EA order risks being unenforceable or set aside. The pack's default (foreign seat only; India seat → Registrar legal review; flag `intl.ea_ppo_india_seat` OFF) is appropriate. | Residual: Case law on ex parte arbitral interim orders in India.. Gate: `intl.ea_ppo_india_seat` stays OFF; India-seat urgent ex parte needs go to court under s.9.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J2-238 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V6_INT_International_Commercial.md:603: A consent award by a tribunal constituted solely to record a settlement already reached (App. A B05) is valid and enforceable. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Open legal risk; no Supreme Court authority was found (Appendix A B05 also records none). s.30 contemplates settlement 'during arbitral proceedings'. Where the dispute is settled before the tribunal exists, a court may find no dispute was referred, and a foreign court may deny NYC treatment. Converting also removes the Singapore Convention route (Art. 1(3)(b)). The pack's default (Part III conciliation for domestic pre-arbitration settlements; legal-review flag for the 'constituted for purpose' route) is right. Evidence: [Singapore Convention, Art. 1(3)(b)](https://uncitral.un.org/sites/default/files/media-documents/EN/Texts/UNCITRAL/Arbitration/mediation_convention_v1900316_eng.pdf) | Residual: Indian and foreign case law on post-settlement tribunals.. Gate: `tribunal_constituted_for_purpose` only with an existing arbitration agreement covering a dispute that is live when the tribunal is appointed, and legal review; copy warns that conversion ends Singapore Convention eligibility.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J2-239 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V6_INT_International_Commercial.md:917: Workflow step 6c: s.73 settlement → s.74 status; cross-border recognition. | **counsel sign-off** (open) | Same analysis as J2-233. Award status in India (s.74) is clear. Abroad, NYC treatment is doubtful, and Singapore Convention Art. 1(3)(b) may exclude it because it is enforceable as an arbitral award. Step 6c should state 'India enforcement only' and steer cross-border cases to step 6a (consent award) or 6b (Art. 4 attestation). The two are alternatives: converting a settlement into an award takes it outside the Singapore Convention. Evidence: [Singapore Convention, Art. 1(3) and Art. 4(1)(b)](https://uncitral.un.org/sites/default/files/media-documents/EN/Texts/UNCITRAL/Arbitration/mediation_convention_v1900316_eng.pdf) | Residual: As J2-233. Gate: As J2-233. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |
| J2-240 | Legal counsel → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V6_INT_International_Commercial.md:1027: GV-INT-11 computes the Hague-Visby last day as 15 Mar 2027 for delivery on 15 Mar 2026 (General Clauses Act first-day exclusion). | **counsel sign-off** (open) | The Schedule text is 'within one year after delivery' (Art. III para 6(c)). Excluding the day of delivery (General Clauses Act 1897 s.9; Limitation Act s.12(1)) gives 15 Mar 2027 as the last day, so the vector is consistent with Indian convention. But the time bar is a substantive contractual/statutory bar often litigated abroad, and foreign courts may count differently. A conservative 'safe date' one day earlier (14 Mar 2027) avoids the risk. Evidence: [Carriage of Goods by Sea Act 2025, Schedule Art. III para 6(c), India ](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/21917/1/A2025-19.pdf) | Residual: Foreign-law computation and treatment of non-working days.. Gate: Show 'indicative last day 15 Mar 2027 (Indian convention); file by 14 Mar 2027 to be safe'; never 'time-barred'.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |

| J2-256 | CFO / Registrar → CFO with tax adviser | appendices/Q_Privacy_Security_and_Certification_Readiness.md:58: RBI card-on-file tokenisation circular is RBI/2021-22/86, DPSS.CO.PD No.1463/02.14.003/2021-22, 7 Sep 2021. | **corrected** (**closed**) | Wrong reference. The RBI site gives RBI/2021-22/96, CO.DPSS.POLC.No.S-516/02-14-003/2021-22, 7 Sep 2021, 'Tokenisation – Card Transactions: Permitting Card-on-File Tokenisation (CoFT) Services'. DPSS.CO.PD No.1463/02.14.003/2018-19 (8 Jan 2019) is an earlier tokenisation circular that this one cites. The circular bars storing actual card data by anyone in the chain other than issuers and networks. The original date was 1 Jan 2022, later extended, which is the basis of the pack's 1 Oct 2022 date (extension notices not opened today). Tokens need explicit consent plus AFA. Only the last four digits and issuer name may be kept. Evidence: [RBI — Tokenisation – Card Transactions: Permitting CoFT Services (7 Se](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12159&Mode=0) | Residual: Citation of the extension circular/press release to 30 Sep 2022; PA Directions 2025 paragraph numbers.. Views differ across High Courts. | Residual: High Court rulings on execution forum.. Gate: FR-2 output names both the Commercial Court and the principal civil court, with 'your advocate will confirm the forum'.. Recheck: On counsel opinion. |

| J2-267 | CFO / Registrar → Registrar / General Counsel | verticals/V1_FIN_Financial_and_Loan.md:828: Day-count convention for gold-release compensation (C-FIN-06). | **provisional** (open) | 'Working days' is not defined in RBI/2025-26/47; para 7 falls back to the parent Acts, RBI regulations or commercial usage. Compensation is '₹5,000 for each day of delay', with days unqualified, so calendar days is the natural reading. The pack's method (due = 7 working days after repayment using the RE's branch calendar; delay counted in calendar days) is sound. Worked example checked: Mon 1 Feb 2027 + 7 working days = Wed 10 Feb; released Tue 16 Feb = 6 days = ₹30,000. Evidence: [RBI (Lending Against Gold and Silver Collateral) Directions 2025, RBI/](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12859); [NBFC RBC Directions 2025 text, RBI/DOR/2025-26/362 (TaxGuru reproducti](https://taxguru.in/?p=1015048) | Residual: Whether Saturdays count as working days depends on the RE's branch calendar; this should be a tenant config.. | J2-268 | CFO / Registrar → Registrar / General Counsel | verticals/V1_FIN_Financial_and_Loan.md:831: Apportionment of CIC-complaint compensation between CI and CIC (C-FIN-09). | **corrected** (**closed**) | The pack's formula is right only for complaints filed with the CI. Per the RBI/2023-24/72 Annex: (a) for complaints filed with a CIC, the CI's 21 days run from the date the CIC informs the CI, not from the complaint (Case 2); (b) for complaints filed with a CI, delay in final delivery by the CI after the CIC's report is the CI's (Cases 6b, 6d); (c) several CIs share their part pro rata to their own delay (Cases 4a–4c). The day of filing is day 0. Pack example matches Case 7. Evidence: [RBI/2023-24/72 with Annex (PDICAI copy)](https://www.pdicai.org/Docs/RBI-2023-24-72_3010202312055997.PDF); [RBI/2023-24/72 CIC compensation framework](https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12554) | Residual: None; implement the Annex cases as unit tests.. | J2-274 | CFO / Registrar → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:235: ISA Ch. II (Christians etc.): widow 1/3 with lineal descendants, 1/2 with kindred (s.33); s.33A ₹5,000 charge — applicability to Indian Christians. | **corrected** (**closed**) | Corrected. Section 33A applies only where the intestate leaves a widow and no lineal descendants. Under s.33A(5)(a) it does not apply to the property of any Indian Christian, of a child or grandchild of a male Indian Christian, or of certain Hindu/Buddhist/Sikh/Jaina persons married under the 1872 Act. Under s.33A(5)(b) it does not apply unless the deceased was wholly intestate. The calculator must not add the ₹5,000 charge for Indian Christian estates. Evidence: [Indian Succession Act 1925 s.33A (bare act, AdvocateKhoj)](https://www.advocatekhoj.com/library/bareacts/indiansuccession/6.php) | Residual: Bare-act text from a secondary host; recheck wording on India Code before release.. Gate: Calculator: s.33A branch OFF for religion = Christian.. Recheck: Before calculator release. |
| J2-275 † | CFO / Registrar → CFO with tax adviser | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:249: Succession certificate (ISA Part X): court fee per state law — state court-fee rates. | **product decision** (open) | Not closed. Court fee for a succession certificate is an ad valorem percentage of the debts and securities, set by each state's Court-fees Act or its state amendment of the 1870 Act. Examples are the Court Fees (Delhi Amendment) Act 2012 and the Maharashtra Court-fees Act. Rates differ and change. Recommendation: keep a per-state `court_fee_succession_certificate` table, show 'estimate — confirm with the District Court', and have state rates signed off before publication. Evidence: [Court Fees (Delhi Amendment) Act, 2012 (vLex)](https://vlex.in/vid/court-fees-delhi-amendment-545547618); [Maharashtra Court-fees Act (LegitQuest)](https://www.legitquest.com/act/maharashtra-court-fees-act/7264) | Residual: Actual slab rates for Delhi, UP, Rajasthan, Maharashtra, Karnataka; not obtained (search budget exhausted).. Gate: Court-fee estimator OFF until state table signed off; text says 'court fee per state law'.. Recheck: Before FAM probate pack launch. |
| J2-276 | CFO / Registrar → General Counsel / external counsel | verticals/V3_FAM_Family_Marital_and_Property.md:282: MWPSC s.6(6): Tribunal may refer to a Conciliation Officer before hearing; findings within one month; Conciliation Officer includes a person nominated by the Tribunal — Explanation text and state rules. | **verified** (**closed**) | Verified on India Code. Under s.6(6) the Tribunal may, before hearing a s.5 application, refer it to a Conciliation Officer, who submits findings within one month. If a settlement is reached the Tribunal passes an order. The Explanation defines a Conciliation Officer as any person or representative of an organisation referred to in the Explanation to s.5(1), the Maintenance Officers designated under s.18(1), or any other person the Tribunal nominates. Equator is therefore eligible only if nominated. State rules were not checked for each state. Evidence: [India Code — Maintenance and Welfare of Parents and Senior Citizens Ac](https://indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19405/1/maintenance_and_welfare_of_parents_and_senior_citizens_act%2c2007.pdf) | Residual: State MWPSC Rules (Delhi, UP, Rajasthan, Maharashtra, Karnataka) on conciliation panels and fees.. Gate: B2G mode fam.mwpsc_conciliation.v1 only on written Tribunal nomination.. Recheck: Before B2G pilot in any state. |
