## J.6 Disposition fields and closure rule (adopted 22 October 2026, D2-29)

Every open item carries the fields management proposed on 22 October 2026, mapped as follows:

| Field (management proposal) | Where it sits | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| Disposition | Register column 4 | One of the seven values in the table below |
| Primary source or evidence link | "Evidence" in column 5 | A link the researcher actually opened, or one returned by search; reproductions are named as such |
| Date checked | 22 Oct 2026 for this pass; later passes add their own date | Recorded per item in `core.legal_rules.verified_on` when the item feeds a rule |
| Exact proposition verified | Column 3 | Stated as a testable sentence |
| Residual uncertainty | Column 6 | Never left blank for a non-closed item |
| Owner and approver | Column 2 | Owner researches or acts; approver signs off |
| Production gate or fallback | Column 6 | The flag or behaviour that applies until closure |
| Recheck date / trigger | Column 6 | Watch items are reopened automatically by the regulatory watch job (P00) when the trigger fires |

| Disposition | Meaning | Closes the item? |
|---|---|---|
| `verified` | A primary source confirms the proposition as written in the pack | Yes |
| `corrected` | A primary source shows the pack was wrong; the pack is fixed | Yes |
| `provisional` | Best answer on secondary sources, or primary source unavailable; product default labelled "Equator policy" | No: recheck required |
| `counsel sign-off` | Interpretation or application needs a written opinion; research note and interim default recorded | No: closes on a written opinion |
| `vendor confirmation` | Needs account- or contract-specific evidence; public documentation recorded | No: closes on written vendor evidence or contract |
| `product decision` | Internal choice; recommendation recorded | No: closes on a recorded decision by the approver |
| `watch` | Draft, consultation, or commencement pending | No: reopened on trigger |

**Closure rule.**
- An item is closed only on `verified` or `corrected`, or on written sign-off by its approver.
- No item is deleted. Superseded items remain with a pointer to the item that closed them (e.g., J-074 → J2-235).
- "Not found" is never treated as "does not exist": the finding records what was searched.

**Data model.** The register is loaded into `core.open_items` (P00 v2.1 migration) with these fields, linked to `core.legal_rules` and `core.feature_flags`. The P14 go-live checklist reads the launch gates in §J.5.2 from it.

## J.7 Response to management's comments of 22 October 2026

Management sent comments on the register, prepared with another AI tool, together with a master product prompt. Our assessment:

**1. Overall approach: agreed and adopted.**
- The register is now a triage and sign-off register with traceable dispositions (§J.6; D2-29).
- Conservative product rules are labelled "Equator policy", not law.
- Vendor facts are separated from contract evidence.
- Commencement-dependent items sit on a dated watch list.

**2. The count of 469 entries is correct** (J-001–J-170 and J2-001–J2-299). Three references in the comments point to IDs that do not exist in the v1 series: "J-290" and "J-274–J-275" are **J2-290** (face-match bands) and **J2-274/J2-275** (Indian Succession Act s.33/s.33A; succession-certificate court fees). The register uses the J2 IDs.

**3. Specific claims checked against primary sources:**

| Comment | Our finding | Effect on the pack |
|---|---|---|
| A. IR Code s.42 needs a written agreement in a prescribed form; keep the feature off until rules confirm the form | **Correct, but now out of date for the Central sphere.** s.42(1)–(8) confirmed on India Code. The IR (Central) Rules 2026 (G.S.R. 342(E), 8 May 2026) r.20 prescribe **Form V**, with the arbitrator's written consent, copied to the conciliation officer and the Secretary, Ministry of Labour. State forms vary: UP and Rajasthan reported final, Maharashtra in draft, Delhi and Karnataka unconfirmed | Central sphere may go live after counsel approves the Form V template; each State stays OFF until its form is verified (LG-05) |
| B. Mediation Act commencement is provision-by-provision; s.12A authorisation is a gate; do not claim s.12A compliance | **Correct.** In force: ss.1, 3, 26, 31–38, 45–47, 50–54 and 56–57 (S.O. 4384(E), 9 Oct 2023). S.O. 4781(E) of 27 Aug 2026 only established the Mediation Council of India. Under CCA s.12A only Legal Services Authorities are authorised (S.O. 3232(E), 3 Jul 2018); the amended s.12A(2)(ii) is not in force | Unchanged position (M-06, LR-MED-019). **Additional correction:** the MSEFC referral to a mediation service provider comes from Mediation Act s.62 and the Seventh Schedule, as amended by MSMED (Amendment) Act 2026 s.8, which has **no commencement date yet**; the original MSMED s.18 still operates (D2-26 corrected) |
| C. DPDP commencement must be cited; build controls now | **Correct.** G.S.R. 843(E), 13 Nov 2025: s.44(1) and (3) in force from 13 Nov 2025; **s.44(2) (omission of IT Act s.43A) from 13 May 2027**, with most fiduciary duties. DPDP Rules G.S.R. 846(E). A Removal of Difficulties Order (S.O. 5458(E), 5 Oct 2026) changes wording only | LR-DPDP-001 and master X-37 corrected; SPDI Rules apply until 13 May 2027 |
| D. Appellate arbitration tier behind a gate pending counsel | **Correct.** *Centrotrade* upholds two-tier arbitration; no Supreme Court ruling settles the s.34(3) trigger or s.29A for the appellate tribunal | Appeal tier OFF in production (LG-07); default: s.34 clock from the final award, with a protective warning from first-tier receipt |
| E. Do not encode a policy suit-bar as a universal limitation rule | **Correct, and the law is clearer than the comment suggests.** Contract Act s.28(b) (from 8 Jan 1997) voids clauses that extinguish rights on expiry of a period (*IndusInd Bank* (2016) 9 SCC 720; *Oriental Insurance v Sanjesh*, 2022). Pre-1997 policies differ | Clock labelled "contestable"; never blocks a claim |
| F. Telecom: cite the final Third Amendment and its commencement | **Correct.** Reg. 05 of 2026, dated 18 Sep 2026. Its e-Gazette ID carries 28 Sep 2026, so the publication date is uncertain. Commencement runs in 30/60/90-day tiers: reg 21A in the 30-day tier; the A2P declaration under reg 4 in the 60-day tier; the reg 25 changes in the 90-day tier | LR-TEL-010 corrected (nothing commenced on 18 Sep). A2P declarations are planned for **before 17 Nov 2026**, using the earlier date (LG-03) |
| G. POSH 90-day start point is unsettled | **Correct.** The statute gives no start point | Complaint-receipt default kept, labelled provisional (LG-06) |
| H. Vendor security records before production | **Agreed.** Public documentation: Sarvam sends a static token header; Exotel and MSG91 callbacks are unsigned; Digio signs with `X-Digio-Checksum` (HMAC-SHA256); ElevenLabs India base URL confirmed and its simulate-conversation API deprecated (use Agent Testing) | Integration record required per adapter (LG-18) |
| I. Do not call a provider hold "escrow" | **Correct.** RazorpayX Escrow+ is a bank escrow with a trustee; a payment-gateway hold is not escrow | Wording rule in App. U §U.2.8; `ESCROW_PORT` OFF until contracts are executed (LG-09) |
| J. No automated rejection on face-match; Bar roll and BCI r.36 limits | **Correct.** BCI Certificate and Place of Practice Rules 2015 r.9.1: 5-year validity and verification cycle. No State Council publishes automated-use terms. The BCI r.36 list is narrower than the pack's advocate profile | No-auto-reject rule; per-Council permission; advocate profiles trimmed (LG-10, LG-11) |
| K. State-specific stamp and registration logic | **Agreed.** Maharashtra Art. 12 (as amended by Ord. XII of 2024) still excludes partition awards; the UP ₹5,000 partition-deed cap is reported but its notification was not found; Delhi and Rajasthan are open | Verified jurisdiction matrix only (LG-14) |

**4. Findings the comments did not cover, but which change the pack:**
1. RBI recovery-contact hours are **already in force**, and a microfinance 09:00–18:00 window applies (LG-02).
2. Recovery-agency risk for lender-paid programmes (LG-01).
3. The companion CIC circular is **RBI/2023-24/73**, not /71. The CIC compensation calculator follows the Annex to RBI/2023-24/72.
4. **SEBI SMART ODR**: both parties already pay arbitration fees under the master circular (para 28(c)). Appendix B and LR-SEBI-006 are corrected.
5. ***Snidha Mehra v Union of India*** (SC, 19 Nov 2025): Hindu Succession Act s.15 pre-litigation mediation goes only to court-annexed centres or Legal Services Authorities (LG-19).
6. **Singapore Convention Art. 1(3)(b)**: converting a mediated settlement into a consent award or a s.74 settlement takes it outside the Convention. The two routes are alternatives (V6).
7. **NCLT/NCLAT formats** need double spacing and different margins from the Supreme Court profile.
8. **IR Code references:**
   - definition letters are "worker" s.2(zr) and "wages" s.2(zq);
   - the Grievance Redressal Committee cap is s.4(4) and its period s.4(6);
   - the re-skilling fund remittance is due **10 days** after retrenchment (r.37).
9. **Other citations corrected:**
   - IT (Intermediary) Amendment Rules on synthetic content: **G.S.R. 120(E), 10 Feb 2026**, binding on intermediaries;
   - RBI card-on-file circular: **RBI/2021-22/96**;
   - Repealing and Amending Act 2025: **Act No. 37 of 2025**;
   - CERT-In Directions (i)–(iv): verified.
10. **Competitor and ecosystem facts:**
    - Insurance Samadhan now charges ₹999 + 20% + GST;
    - Sahamati has no "Vikalp" programme (it is the Inter-Member dispute resolution framework; renamed throughout the pack);
    - A&C s.31A(5) voids pre-dispute "costs in any event" clauses, so they are barred from Equator templates.

**5. The master product prompt** pasted with the comments has the same structure as the 21 October prompt. Requirements R-01–R-40 (`02` §2) already cover it, with one new element: "negotiation participants or facilitators". That is added as R-43, the `neutral_facilitator` role (App. S §6.2; App. U §U.2.2).

**6. Old Equa website.** Its institutional rules, code of conduct, privacy policy, terms, refund policy, disclaimer, security and accessibility pages, and model clauses have been used as the base of Appendix U. Five Code rules have been added to ECC-2026.1, and 17 risky items are listed for removal (App. U §U.12).

## J.9 Configurability of open items (v3.0, 23 October 2026)

From v3.0 every open item in §J.5.3 is also classified by **configurability** under NN-33 (three-tier configuration). The full classification — category, tier, settings key or rule pack, who may change the value, and its default or configured fallback — is **Appendix V §V.3** (§V.3.4 register, 469 rows; one row per item, no item omitted). This appendix remains the record of evidence, disposition, approver, production gate and recheck trigger (§J.5–§J.6, D2-29); Appendix V decides the tier and the key (Appendix V precedence 3).

| Tier (NN-33) | Items | Meaning for an open item |
|---|---|---|
| **L** — Locked | 65 | The interim position is protective and is held in code (statutory exclusions, prohibitions, NN-linked blocks, copy lints). Closing the item can only *relax* it, by a platform release with an ADR and Head of Legal approval |
| **G** — Governed | 346 | A legal or regulatory parameter, rule pack or legal flag, changed through `core.config_change_requests` (maker ≠ checker, citation, effective date, review date, fallback); 17 of them are tenant-adaptable within bounds by the tenant's legal reviewer |
| **T** — Tenant | 7 | Tenant-supplied values (chargeback windows, HFC escalation text, scheme limits, working calendar, invitation deemed-rejection days) |
| **N** — No runtime setting | 51 | Citations, legends and research notes; no runtime behaviour waits on them |
| **Total** | **469** | Counts confirmed against the §V.3.4 register on 23 October 2026 (§V.3.3) |

Rules:
1. **Link to configuration.** `core.open_items` gains `config_category`, `tier`, `cfg_key`, `rule_pack`, `changed_by` and `fallback_text` (master X-46). Closing an item opens a G change request that moves the key from its fallback to its final value; the P14 go-live checklist reads launch gates from both tables.
2. **Fallback is mandatory.** Every G item declares a configured fallback that applies automatically and visibly while the item is unverified, unapproved or past its review date (D3-13); 81 rows keep a feature OFF until closure (§V.3.3).
3. **Production gates stand.** A §J.5 production gate is not lifted by configuration: a G value can move off its fallback only when the gate's approver signs off (D2-29).
4. **v3.0 correction recorded here.** J-159, J-162, J-167 and LR-TAX-007: the e-commerce-operator TDS under Income-tax Act 2025 s.393(1) Table Sl. 8(v) is **not** "nil threshold": a resident individual/HUF participant is excluded up to ₹5 lakh in the tax year if PAN or Aadhaar is furnished, with no threshold for others ([RE] F3.4). Appendix G LR-TAX-007, Appendix B §B.10 and P05 FR-P05-23 are corrected; the flags are `billing.eco_withholding` and `billing.eco_gst_tcs` (`tax.eco_tds`, `tax.eco_gst_tcs` are aliases; J-159 re-keyed in Appendix V).
5. **v3 open items join the same register.** *(v3.1)* The v3 files carry their own open items (for example `AA-OI-nn`, `W-OI-nn`, `X-OI-nn`, `CFG-OI-nn`, `P19-OI-nn`, `P20-OI-nn`, Appendix Z §Z.20 items) and further **[VERIFY — open: …]** markers. They are loaded into `core.open_items` with `series = 'V3'`, keeping their native IDs; a marker without a native ID receives `J3-nnn` from the loader in file order (stable once assigned; the source file and anchor are stored). Each carries the D2-29 fields (§J.6). Its tier, key and fallback come from the owning appendix's settings table (not from Appendix V §V.3, which stays the register of the 469 v1–v2 items). CI fails if the number of `V3` rows differs from the number of markers and native IDs found in V–AF and P15–P21.
6. **One launch-gate list.** The P14 go-live checklist and each v3 phase's exit gate read LG-01–LG-19 (§J.5.2) and LG-V3-01–LG-V3-12 (§J.9.1) from `core.open_items`; a gate is green only when every linked item is closed under §J.6.
7. **v3.0 supersessions recorded.** *(v3.1)* Some v1–v2 items now sit under binding v3 rules; they stay in the register (no item is deleted, §J.6) but no feature waits on them any longer: **J-107** (non-cash referral credits to advocates) — the interim default became a locked rule: no referral reward of any kind to advocates, CAs, CSs or CMAs (NN-17, X-62, D3-27); the item remains open only on whether any reward could ever be permitted, and closing it cannot relax X-62 without a new decision. **J-083** and **J2-090** (advocate-neutral profile fields) — governed by LR-PROF-001 and NN-39 (Schedule particulars only), feeding LG-11 and LG-V3-03. **J-109** (E-Commerce Rules 2020 for Equator's own online services) — the marketplace duties are LR-CONS-012; the item stays open for Equator's own-service sales. **J2-002**, **J2-042** and **J2-045** (Grade B label "Senior Neutral") — decided by master X-26: the public labels are Distinguished / Principal / Associate Neutral and copy lint blocks "Senior" as a tier label; Appendices N, P, T and `02` D2-12 use "Principal Neutral" from v3.1, so the sub-label gate no longer applies; J2-002 can close under §J.6 with X-26 as the decision record, and J2-042/J2-045 stay open only for the Hindi label (V31-OI-10). *(v3.1)*

### J.9.2 Working the register: open-item workflow *(v3.1)*

The register is worked in the Configuration Studio's **Rules and legal → Open items** view (P15), over `core.open_items`:

- **States.** `new → in_research → awaiting_external (counsel · vendor · board · regulator) → ready_for_signoff → closed`; side states `reopened` (a watch trigger fired or new evidence arrived) and `superseded` (pointer to the closing item, §J.6). `closed` needs the §J.6 closure rule; where the item has a G key, closing opens the change request of rule 1 (P15 FR-P15-59 "Apply final value").
- **Ordering.** Launch-gate items first (LG-01–LG-19, LG-V3-01–12), then by the earliest go-live date of a phase that depends on the item (from the phase plans), then by age. Saved views: "My items", "Awaiting counsel", "Chase overdue", "Blocking next release".
- **Timers.** A new item is acknowledged by its owner within 5 working days; an `awaiting_external` item carries a chase date (default every 14 days, T `tenant.legal.open_items.chase_days`) and escalates to its approver after two missed chases; a `reopened` item takes the trigger's 5-working-day SLA (Appendix G §G.4.3).
- **Leave and hand-over.** When an owner is on leave for 5 working days or more, or leaves the organisation, open items move to the cover named on the leave form or to the department head, with a hand-over note; no item is left without an owner (NN-04 pattern).
- **Bulk actions.** Reassign owner; set or move chase dates; attach one evidence record to several items; export the filtered list as XLSX for counsel (NN-37 naming rules).
- **KPIs.** Launch-gate items open; median age of open items by owner; share of `awaiting_external` items with an overdue chase; items closed per month; reopened items per quarter. Shown on the Legal and Compliance department dashboard.
- **Acceptance.** Given a launch-gate item in `awaiting_external` with a missed second chase, when the escalation job runs, then the approver receives a task and the item shows "Escalated"; given an item whose owner starts 10 days' leave, then the item moves to the named cover with an audit event and the cover sees it in "My items".
